## 0. WHY THIS PASS EXISTS

The SF2 pass (all nine seats but Fable, who fires last) found a FLOOR BREACH in revision 3, measured by the Opus seat:
R6 built each re-arm cell on ONE timer, so `==` for `>=` on every timer but timer 2 (M_eq_n2) passed every row in the
battery and left a timer owing 4529 against a bound of 762 at rung 3.  That is the third breach of one class in a row
(`==`, then `pending_bound[0]`, then the timer x re-arm cells), so under this project's rule the repair owes the CLASS
CLOSURE -- enumerate the space, close every cell in one diff, write the contract -- and **this pass VERIFIES THE
ENUMERATION rather than hunting cells.**  The chain closes on the first valid pass that finds ZERO floor breaches in
the shipped composite; a zero must come with what was checked.

## 1. WHAT THE SF2 PASS OVERTURNED, AND WHAT REVISION 4 DID

* F5 ("R6b closes the class") OVERTURNED -- Opus (measured M_eq_n2, M_pend_n0), grok, both codex jobs.  **R6 now builds
  EVERY timer in BOTH re-arm cells** (re-armed at count 0; re-armed owing 8192), each timer re-armed to its OWN rate
  (762/1525/3051/6103), across two structs that swap the roles; it prints owed/peak/END for all eight, and gate 2 pins
  the whole line.  The END count, ((8192-1) mod B)+1, ties each peak to its own timer.
* F6/D4 ("R5 unchanged") OVERTURNED -- codex (a store `(int) bound & ~(timer_nr << 16)` lowers timers 2-3 at the 50 MHz
  case, hidden by R5's maximum), Opus.  **R5 now requires the LOWEST timer's peak to match as well.**
* F14 and F18 were incompletely repaired (the probe header still said "gate 2 -- every arm of it enabled", and "3051,
  identical to D" cannot show the disable executed) -- codex x2.  Rewritten; #548's M_en IS the evidence the disable ran.
* Three sibling wrong records the round had not touched: the harness header's "overflows at a higher emulated_hz", R3's
  "the rung-3 guest probe cannot see the difference at all", the probe's assembler comment "every generated word is still
  checked" -- codex panel.  All corrected.
* The backlog row counted RECORDS, not arms (two D lines passed) -- codex x2.  **Now one D and one X, each in [1, 762].**
* CTRL_ON was unpinned (0x88 -> 0x80 passes every drain row) -- grok, by arithmetic.  **The probe's FBDRAIN_TIMER line
  now carries `load=0x100 ctrl=0x88`, pinned whole.**
* The gate comment's blanket "gate 2's (R1/R2/R3/R5/R6/R6b)" overclaimed -- agy.  **Now per mutant, each to its row.**
* "Clause-(e) ... closed here" still overstated -- grok, minimax.  Now: witnessed on every timer, if X's disable ran.
* The checker's "detectors that NOTHING RUNS" heading was false of all four entries -- agy.  Corrected.
* F28 (upward re-arm) was settled on the wrong mutant; F24/F31's runtime bound is false (wait() resets on output); the
  SF2 packet's CTRL_ON sentence was false -- all recorded in `CORRECTIONS.md` (the dated artifacts stay unedited).
* FILED, not rowed: `fbbounddebt` -- a STORE conditioned by a mutant on the count (M_neqB, M_zge; the 1..761 cell is
  worth a third cell) or on direction (M_upskip, measured passing everything), and an index read from another field
  (`nr & (timer_load[nr]-1)`, codex; `timer_control[nr] & TIMER_FCLK_256 ? nr : 0`, grok); `fbharnessedits` -- edits
  to the probe's or the harness's own logic that keep the pins green (codex dedicated's eight-row table, the kimi seat's
  X-for-D substitution, Opus's three-token R6 edit); `fbboundmodes`, `fbdrainxdisable`, `fbdrainonepass`, `asanstaleobjs`.

## 2. THE ENUMERATION REVISION 4 CLAIMS (verify THIS)

`footbridge_pending(d, nr)`'s guard reads `pending_timer_interrupts[nr]` and `pending_bound[nr]`; `nr` is a floor axis
because it indexes both.  The body tests one partition: count < bound, count >= bound.
* **R6 (re-arm):** {timer 0..3} x {re-armed at count 0 (below), re-armed owing 8192 (at/above)} = 8 cells, all rowed,
  every timer to a distinct new rate.  Downward re-arms (50 MHz -> slower).
* **R6b (first arm):** {timer 0..3} armed first, all at distinct rates, then driven: cross-slot stores and reads show.
* **R5 (the bound's value):** every timer, at seven (hz, load, control) cases; lowest and highest peak must both match.
* **Not rowed, filed:** store guards a mutant ADDS (count == B, 0 < count < B, upward re-arm); index expressions reading a
  field other than `nr`; control values no instrument drives the callback with (PERIODIC, FCLK_16, EXTERNAL).

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

1320:> re-opened the very defect the round had just closed. (b) restores the DEFECT; (d) neuters
1321:> the FIX. A detector can be sensitive to one and blind to the other, because they fail at
1322:> different sites: (b) fails where the old code was, (d) fails where the new code is.
1323:>
1324:> **HOW TO BUILD THE (d) MUTANT �?" and it is the same discriminator the accept-row rule
1325:> already uses.** Take each predicate the fix **DEPENDS ON** and weaken it to the
1326:> least-restrictive form that still *reads* like the same check: a mask narrowed
1327:> (`& 3` -> `& 1`), a condition qualified (`if (X)` -> `if (X && writeflag == MEM_WRITE)`),
1328:> a bound relaxed. **Not deletion** �?" deletion is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes and is usually
1329:> already covered. The test is whether a plausible future edit could produce it.
1330:>
1331:> **WHY THE ONE-CHARACTER FORM IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** `dma alias`, the row written
1332:> specifically to pin that repair, was **STRUCTURALLY BLIND** to it: it used an ODD address,
1333:> which both guard forms drop. *The row aimed at the defect could not see the defect return.*
1334:> A row written from the FIX's intent tests the intent, not the boundary �?" so (d)'s mutant
1335:> has to come from an adversary, which is exactly what the ask-again buys.
1336:>
1337:> **COST: one mutant per repair predicate**, the same order as the accept-row rule (#448 = 4,
1338:> #449 = 1, #461 = 4 across two arms). It is bounded by the diff, so it cannot become #392:
1339:> the predicates are the ones the fix ADDED, and that list is finite and visible.
1340:>
1341:> ### *** "THE PREDICATE THE FIX INTRODUCED" ENUMERATED THE EMPTY SET FOR MOST OF THIS
1342:> ### QUEUE, AND NOBODY NOTICED FOR THREE ROUNDS. Corrected 2026-08-24 -- clause (d) now
1343:> ### reads DEPENDS ON, not INTRODUCED. ***
1344:>
1345:> Clause (d) says *keep the guard, null its repair*. As written it enumerated only
1346:> predicates the diff ADDS -- and **the two commonest fix shapes here add none**:
1347:>
1348:> * a **pure deletion** introduces no predicate at all;
1349:> * a **DE-FATALISATION** introduces none either -- it changes what an EXISTING
1350:>   predicate's branch does. That is most of this project's queue.
1351:>
1352:> So for those rounds clause (d) enumerated **nothing**, and a round could satisfy the
1353:> floor while having built no (d) mutant whatever. `#461` measured (d) successfully only
1354:> because its fix happened to ADD a predicate (an alignment mask) for the mutant to weaken.
1355:>
1356:> **Reading it as DEPENDS ON fixes both cases at once**, and it is why this now replaces a
1357:> narrower deletion-only rule written earlier the same day: that rule was a special case of
1358:> this gap, and two overlapping statements of one rule is the accumulation this carrier is
1359:> supposed to refuse.
1360:>
1361:> **The two shapes, once the enumeration is right:**
1362:> * **fix ADDS a predicate** -> weaken the added one (unchanged; the original rule).
1363:> * **fix adds none** -> the (d) mutant reroutes an EXISTING guard's repair. For a
1364:>   de-fatalisation: **swap the guard's operand for a field the guarded BODY reads.**
1365:>   For a pure deletion: **re-introduce the removed behaviour under a DIFFERENT predicate**
1366:>   -- not the same one widened, which is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes.
1367:>
1368:> *** THE MEASURED CASE, and it is why this matters rather than being tidy: ***
1369:> `#462` de-fatalised two arms. Its guard reads `d->data`; the body it guards reads
1370:> `d->data` AND `d->data_len`; the two are set together in one place and cleared in two
1371:> places that touch only ONE of them. Swapping the guard's operand to `d->data_len == 0`
1372:> **scored a CLEAN SWEEP on a 52-row detector that had just passed twelve mutants**, and
1373:> restored a NULL-pointer read of 408 bytes. Under the old wording that was coverage debt;
1374:> under DEPENDS ON it is a **floor breach, L2, fix in-round** -- which is what the round
1375:> did, before the wording was corrected.
1376:>
1377:> #### *** THE BOUND, and it is not optional: "DEPENDS ON" INVITES TRANSITIVE DATA-FLOW
1378:> #### CHASING, which is unbounded and is how this becomes `#392`. Named 2026-08-24 by the
1379:> #### adjudicating seat, in the same breath as upholding the correction. ***
1380:>
1381:> > **DEPENDS ON enumerates exactly two things: (1) the predicates DOMINATING the diff's
1382:> > edited lines, walked up only to the ENCLOSING FUNCTION BOUNDARY -- never into callees
1383:> > or callers; and (2) for operand-swap forms, swaps drawn ONLY from fields the guarded
1384:> > BODY reads (the guard-field/body-field grep the pre-table check already mandates).**
1385:>
1386:> That is finite, derivable from the diff by reading, and **it provably contains the
1387:> measured case**: the `d->data == NULL` guard dominates the edited decline (clause 1), and
1388:> `data_len` is a body-read field (clause 2), so `M11` falls out mechanically rather than
1389:> by inspiration. It cannot become `#392` because the enumeration stays closed over the
1390:> diff's own hunks -- the same closure the INTRODUCED wording had, without the empty-set
1391:> defect that made INTRODUCED enumerate nothing for a de-fatalisation.
1392:>
1393:> #### *** JOINT CELLS -- adjudicated 2026-09-04 on `pcc2widthkills` pass 10 by the flagship seat, after
1394:> #### a measuring seat's CONJUNCTION of two body-read counts escaped 190/190 AND THE SAME SEAT ARGUED IT
1395:> #### WAS NOT A BREACH.  Both proposed bounds were rejected; the one adopted came from neither. ***
1396:>
1397:> > **DEPENDS-ON enumerates JOINT cells over the guard's operands and the body-read lvalues EXACTLY
1398:> > WHEN THE GUARDED BODY'S OWN CODE DISTINGUISHES THEM.**  For each lvalue take the PARTITION the
1399:> > body's own tests induce (`pending > 0` gives {0, >0}; `relative_addr == PCCTWO_T1ICR` gives
1400:> > {T1, T2}); the enumerated cells are the CROSS-PRODUCT of those partitions -- never arbitrary
1401:> > boolean functions of the read-set.  A mutant exempting a single cell whose reachability is
1402:> > MEASURED is a floor breach; close the whole cross-product in ONE diff and write into the
1403:> > contract which cells are rowed.  A value or combination the body never tests as a case
1404:> > (`idata == k`; a byte no conditional distinguishes; both counts >= 2 where the body tests `> 0`)
1405:> > is DISCLOSED under clause (e).
1406:>
1407:> **Why this form and not the two that were argued.**  The dissent's discriminator -- *a plausible
1408:> future edit* -- failed three ways: plausibility is not derivable by reading; the mutant was written
1409:> and measured, so it is an edit someone writes; and "single-operand is complete" is measurably
1410:> false, since `&& idata != 2` is single-operand and escapes.  The main loop's own bound -- *pairs of
1411:> body-read lvalues over a finite reachable space* -- was rejected as SELF-REFUTING: the counts' raw
1412:> value space is 2^32 apiece, so "finite" does no bounding work on its motivating case (a C `int` is
1413:> finite -- the first wording here said it was not; codex and glm caught it on pass 11); "reachable
1414:> guest state" would force rowing
1415:> `idata == 2` that the same record discloses; and a PAIRS cap misses `#499`'s four axes.  The
1416:> body-tested-partition form is finite (a product of small partition counts), derivable from the
1417:> diff by reading the body's own `if`s, and it enumerates `#499`'s width x component x direction x
1418:> position cells from `len`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr` -- which neither argued bound reached.
1419:>
1420:> **The measured case.**  The TxICR body tests both pending counts at `> 0` and the label at
2260:> measured case is instructive about what waiting buys: three successive reversals landed
2261:> almost exactly where the position stood before the detour -- **but with the warrants
2262:> transformed**. The churn cost adjudication text and zero code, because nothing was committed
2263:> between reversals. *The hold discipline is what made the reversals free.*
2264:>
2265:> ### THE COMPANION, and it is the reason that ruling was defeatable at all
2266:>
2267:> The refuted warrant had three elements. The adjudicator checked the one it suspected
2268:> (*"is the announced event common and benign?"*), found it non-load-bearing, and shipped. The
2269:> vulnerable element was a different one (*"does the diagnostic announce something REAL?"*) --
2270:> **and the fact that defeated it was already in that round's own record, filed under another
2271:> heading four findings earlier.**
2272:>
2273:> > **BEFORE TRANSFERRING A PRECEDENT, GREP THE ROUND'S OWN FINDINGS AGAINST EACH WARRANT
2274:> > ELEMENT.** A warrant checked only against the facts you have in hand can be defeated by a
2275:> > fact you already hold under a different label.
2276:
2277:> **Above floor, measured escapes are COVERAGE DEBT: FILE them**, with the escape list recorded,
2278:> ranked by what they would admit �?" and ranked BELOW live defects in the same file.
2279:
2280:The floor is not invented: it is what four earlier rounds already did. `sh4bcr`'s first detector
2281:passed a mutant reinstating the very host kill its round removed; `sh4pcicexit`'s passed the
2282:original kill verbatim; `pitflip`'s rewind had zero coverage; #446's passed a **217-byte
2283:comment-only file**. All four strengthened or replaced IN-ROUND, and all four were below this
2284:floor. #447's detector is above it, checked row by row �?" so filing its nine escapes was right.
2285:
2286:**Reading "MEASURED FALSE PASS" as "any measured mutant escape" would reproduce #392**: the
2287:measuring seat finds escapes essentially every round and the mutation space is unbounded. The
2288:severity rule settles the ordering �?" #447's escapes are *hypothetical edits in uncovered product
2289:cells*, while `sh4chcr` is a **measured live host death in the shipped tree**.
2290:
2291:> ### *** THE DE-ESCALATION CLAUSE �?" the one genuinely new rule, and it names a hazard this
2292:> project created for itself four times. ***
2293:>
2294:> **A fatal�+'survive fix DELETES THE ACCIDENTAL TRIPWIRE that made guard-mask growth
2295:> self-announcing.** Before #447, adding `| TCR_UNIE` to a guard mask meant `exit(1)` on the
2296:> first legal write �?" unmissable. After it, the same edit means a **silently refused write** and
2297:> nothing anywhere notices.
2298:>
2299:> So: **when a fix removes a host-fatal guard, enumerate from the diff every predicate that used
2300:> to gate death, and the detector shipping with that fix must pin the ACCEPT SIDE of each** �?" a
2301:> row per adjacent modelled bit or value, proving legal writes still land. Those rows are part
2302:> of the FIX'S COST, not future strengthening, because the fix itself destroyed the old
2303:> (accidental) detector for that class.
2304:
2305:It is bounded (the class is exactly "edits the old `exit(1)` would have announced"), and it is
2306:checkable at review time by one question: **does this diff delete `exit(` from a device? then
2307:look for accept rows.** Deliberately NOT mechanised �?" the enumeration needs judgement about
2308:adjacency, and the existing "what is the smallest edit that breaks this and still passes?" seat
2309:invocation already FINDS the instances. What was missing was never detection; it was
2310:DISPOSITION.
2311:
2312:> ### *** ACCEPT-ROW FAILABILITY �?" the companion rule, adopted 2026-08-21 after #449's accept
2313:> row was MEASURED VACUOUS. ***
2314:>
2315:> An accept-side row is **not discharged by existing** �?" it is discharged by a **MEASURED
2316:> KILL**. For each predicate the clause enumerates from the diff, build the smallest
2317:> **realistic JOINT** guard-growth mutant �?" widen the predicate **and** its diagnostic/latch
2318:> operand together, the way adjacent code actually evolves �?" and the accept row must REDDEN
2319:> under it. A row green under its own growth mutant is vacuous whatever the chosen bit's
2320:> legality.
2321:>
2322:> Choose the pinning value so a guest store can leave it **in the predicate's OPERAND**: read
2323:> the store arm, and cite its line in the row's comment. **Write-1-to-keep, write-to-clear,
2324:> read-only and never-set-by-device bits cannot carry an accept row.**
2325:
2326:**"Reaches the predicate's operand" is the checkable notion, and "the guest can produce it" is
2327:one level too shallow** �?" #449's guest *did* write CMF=1 at the interface; the store arm filtered
2328:it before the predicate ever saw it. The measured kill subsumes reachability, and it catches
2329:wrong-regex, wrong-register and wrong-width vacuities in the same row for free.
2330:
2331:*** "JOINT" WAS THE FIRST MEASURED INSTANCE OF THE RULE, NOT THE RULE �?" restated 2026-08-22
2332:by the flagship seat after #452 measured the opposite case. *** #449's guard-only widening was
2333:measured **EQUIVALENT** (the latch argument still masked to the original bits, so nothing
2334:observable happened) and A1's kill only appears when guard and latch widen together. But at
2335:#451's SCI site the latch operand is a compile-time constant the guard cannot feed, so
2336:guard-only and joint redden the accept rows byte for byte �?" and guard-only ADDITIONALLY
2337:silences its own growth, printing one diagnostic where joint prints two. **There, guard-only is
2338:the HARDER mutant, and building only the joint form is the WEAKER test.**
2339:
2340:> **THE RULE, BY ITS DISCRIMINATOR:** the accept row must redden under the **least-observable
2341:> growth form that is measured NON-EQUIVALENT at that site**. Find it by the reading the rule
2342:> already mandates �?" read the latch/store arm and cite its line. Where the latch operand CAN
2343:> mask the guard, guard-only is equivalent and JOINT is that form. Where the latch operand is a
2344:> constant the guard cannot feed, guard-only is non-equivalent AND quieter, so it is that form.
2345:> **"Build the form the latch operand does not mask"** is the generalisation, and it is not a
2560:>
2561:> > **Review EFFORT is scoped to the repair diff, so settled design is not re-litigated. The
2562:> > FLOOR QUESTION -- does a clean-sweep mutant exist in the (d) enumeration -- is asked of the
2563:> > SHIPPED COMPOSITE on every pass.**
2564:>
2565:> #### THE BOUND IS IMPORTED, NOT INTRINSIC -- state the dependency or the rule overclaims
2566:>
2567:> The stop condition does not bound the chain and must not pretend to. The bound comes from
2568:> **the (d) DEPENDS-ON enumeration being diff-closed and finite**, and from *zero breaches*
2569:> meaning **zero SURVIVING ADJUDICATION** rather than zero raised -- otherwise one stubborn
2570:> seat blocks closure forever, against the count-arguments-not-seats rule. With those imports
2571:> the chain is infinite only if every repair is defective, in which case every pass is buying a
2572:> real defect off the ship.
2573:>
2574:> #### *** THE AMENDMENT THAT ACTUALLY ENDS CHAINS, and it is the part worth carrying. ***
2575:>
2576:> `#499`'s three breaches were **THE SAME CLASS THREE TIMES** -- a cell of one state space left
2577:> empty, repaired at exactly the address the mutant had used, leaving the symmetric cell open
2578:> for the next pass. Pass 1 found *width* unpinned; pass 2 found *component*; pass 3 found
2579:> *direction* AND *position*. **Pass-per-instance converges LINEARLY IN CELLS**, and 28 -> 34
2580:> -> 36 rows bought one cell each.
2581:>
2582:> > **WHEN TWO CONSECUTIVE PASSES FIND BREACHES OF ONE CLASS, THE NEXT REPAIR OWES THE CLASS
2583:> > CLOSURE: enumerate the full space from the function's own inputs, close every cell in ONE
2584:> > diff, and write into the CONTRACT which cells are covered and which are deliberately not.
2585:> > The closing pass then VERIFIES THE ENUMERATION rather than hunting cells.**
2586:>
2587:> That is the reading-seat-buys-the-class lesson wearing the repair hat. It also converts a
2588:> future escape from *"another breach"* into *"outside the contract, as stated"* -- which is
2589:> exactly what clause (e) is for, and which a cell-by-cell chain can never reach.
2590:>
2591:> *Provenance: proposed by the main loop, adjudicated by the flagship seat AGAINST ITSELF --
2592:> it was asked to be harder on a rule that favours finishing, and returned two defects and an
2593:> amendment. The measuring seat independently reached the same amendment from the other end:
2594:> "adding two more rows at the corner I just named will produce a fourth pass."*
2595:

2597:2026-08-13 with the full battery never run once; a red battery could not have been attributed
2598:to any of them.
2599:* every commit: its own TARGETED gate (~1 min), in the same commit as the fix;

2607:  wall-clock oracle, so it genuinely needs the machine to itself).

1:/*
2: *  #442 fbpending: an OFFLINE DIRECT-CALL DETECTOR for the pending-interrupt bound.
3: *
4: *  *** THIS IS NOT A REPRODUCTION AND MUST NEVER BE RECORDED AS ONE. ***  Under this
5: *  project's witness ladder it is rung 1: it #includes the device's own translation
6: *  unit and calls timer_tickN() directly, with no machine description, no address
7: *  decode and no device dispatch.  It still compiles and still fails with all of that
8: *  removed, which is the mechanical discriminator the ladder gives.  The reproduction
9: *  is the rung-3 cold-debugger witness; this is the row that DEFENDS the fix.
10: *
11: *  WHY IT IS NEEDED IN ADDITION TO THE RUNG-3 ROW, measured rather than assumed.  The
12: *  rung-3 drain probe measures a backlog by letting DEVICE_TICK deliver ticks the guest
13: *  can see.  Two consequences, both measured against real mutants:
14: *
15: *    - a bound placed in DEVICE_TICK instead of in the callback (mutant m2) is applied
16: *      by the very mechanism the probe uses to observe, so the probe reads a bounded
17: *      counter and passes.  A direct call never runs DEVICE_TICK at all.
18: *    - a bound with no ABSOLUTE ceiling (mutant m6, cap == (int)freq alone) is correct
19: *      at cats' 50 MHz and at a higher emulated_hz lets the debt reach INT_MAX before
20: *      the compare-first reset -- NOT an overflow (#548 measured R2 green with the
21: *      ceiling deleted; this said "overflows" until then).  A guest probe cannot
22: *      reach that in bounded time: the core delivers at most TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP *
23: *      TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY == 68,157,440 increments per wall second, so 2^31 of them
24: *      take 31.5 s AT BEST.  A direct call does 2^31 increments in seconds.
25: *
26: *  It compiles the SHIPPED source, not a copy of it -- a detector that restates the
27: *  code it is testing is the vacuity taxonomy's first entry.
28: */
29:
30:#include "dev_footbridge.c"
31:
32:#include <limits.h>
33:#include <stdarg.h>
34:
35:
36:/*  The four globals main.c owns.  Linking against every object EXCEPT main.o and
37:    dev_footbridge.o is what keeps this detector honest -- it runs the real timer.c
38:    and the real footbridge, not re-implementations -- and these are the only
39:    symbols that dropping main.o leaves undefined.  */
40:struct settings *global_settings;
41:int extra_argc;
42:char **extra_argv;
43:char *progname = (char *) "fbpending_bound";
44:size_t dyntrans_cache_size = DEFAULT_DYNTRANS_CACHE_SIZE;
45:
46:
47:static struct machine fake_machine;
48:static struct cpu fake_cpu;
49:
50:static int failures;
51:
52:
53:/*  #442: the identity row's expected counts, named rather than written inline so a reader
54:    sees WHICH number moved when a row is added or removed.
55:
56:    MEASURED, not predicted.  The first draft carried 18/17 from counting the rows by hand
57:    and both were off by one -- which is the whole argument for this row existing: a count
58:    a human derives is a count that drifts, and here it was wrong before it ever shipped.  */
59:#define IDENT_DEEP     28	/*  with R2, i.e. a depth argument was given  */
60:#define IDENT_SHALLOW  27	/*  without  */
61:
62:static int rows;
63:
64:static void row(const char *name, int good, const char *fmt, ...)
65:{
66:	va_list ap;
67:	printf("  %-4s %-44s ", good ? "ok" : "FAIL", name);
68:	va_start(ap, fmt);
69:	vprintf(fmt, ap);
70:	va_end(ap);
71:	printf("\n");
72:	rows ++;
73:	if (!good)
74:		failures ++;
75:}
76:
77:
78:/*
79: *  Drive one timer through `n` producer ticks and report the high-water mark.
80: *
81: *  The counter is read back after EVERY call rather than only at the end, because the
82: *  interesting failure is transient: a bound that resets to 1 makes the final value
83: *  small even when the peak was INT_MIN.  A final-value-only check would be green on
84: *  the very defect this file exists for.
85: */
86:static void drive(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, long long n,
87:	int *peak, int *trough)
88:{
89:	long long i;
90:	*peak = INT_MIN;
91:	*trough = INT_MAX;
92:	for (i = 0; i < n; i++) {
93:		switch (nr) {
94:		case 0: timer_tick0(NULL, d); break;
95:		case 1: timer_tick1(NULL, d); break;
96:		case 2: timer_tick2(NULL, d); break;
97:		case 3: timer_tick3(NULL, d); break;
98:		}
99:		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] > *peak)
100:			*peak = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
101:		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] < *trough)
102:			*trough = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
103:	}
104:}
105:
106:
107:/*  Arm timer `nr` exactly as a guest would: a control write then a load write,
108:    through the device's own register handler, so the model's own interpretation of
109:    the two registers is what is under test rather than a re-derivation of it.  */
110:static void arm(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, uint32_t load, uint32_t control)
111:{
112:	d->timer_control[nr] = control;
113:	d->timer_load[nr] = load & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
114:	reload_timer_value(&fake_cpu, d, nr);
115:}
116:
117:
118:int main(int argc, char *argv[])
119:{
120:	struct footbridge_data *d;
121:	int peak, trough, nr;
122:	long long deep = 0;
123:	double f;
124:
125:	if (argc > 1)
126:		deep = atoll(argv[1]);
127:
128:	fake_cpu.machine = &fake_machine;
129:	timer_init();
130:
131:	printf("==============================================================\n");
132:	printf("fbpending OFFLINE BOUND DETECTOR (rung 1 -- a DETECTOR, not a\n");
133:	printf("reproduction).  TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP=%d  TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY=%.0f\n",
134:	    TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP, (double)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
135:	printf("  the most one timer can be ticked in one wall second: %.0f\n",
136:	    (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
137:	printf("==============================================================\n");
138:
139:	/*
140:	 *  R0 -- the CONTROL that makes every row below mean something.  If the harness
141:	 *  cannot drive the counter at all, every bound row passes vacuously.  Drive an
142:	 *  ARMED timer 4096 times with the bound deliberately out of reach and require
143:	 *  the counter to actually move.
144:	 */
145:	d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
146:	fake_machine.emulated_hz = 50000000;
147:	arm(d, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);
148:	drive(d, 0, 4096, &peak, &trough);
149:	row("R0 the harness can drive the counter at all", peak >= 4096,
150:	    "peak after 4096 direct ticks = %d (expect >= 4096 with no bound)", peak);
151:	free(d);
152:
153:	/*
154:	 *  R1 -- THE PROPERTY.  For each of the four timers, and for a spread of
155:	 *  guest-reachable (emulated_hz, load, control) triples, the counter must stay
156:	 *  inside [0, ceiling] across more increments than the ceiling itself.
157:	 *
158:	 *  emulated_hz is included in the sweep because it is USER-REACHABLE: main.c:376
159:	 *  sets machine->emulated_hz from -I, and emul_parse.c:621 from a config file.  A
160:	 *  cap of the form (int)freq with no absolute ceiling is correct at 50 MHz and
161:	 *  becomes INT_MAX at the top of that range -- which is mutant m6, and which no
162:	 *  row run only at cats' default would ever see.
163:	 */
164:	{
165:		static const struct { int hz; uint32_t load; uint32_t ctrl;
166:			const char *what; } cases[] = {
167:		    {    50000000, 0x000100, TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256, "cats, load 256/256" },
168:		    {    50000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "cats, load 1  (50 MHz)" },
169:		    {    63750000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "netwinder, load 1" },
170:		    {  1000000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I 1e9, load 1" },
171:		    {  2147483647, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 1" },
172:		    {  2147483647, 0x000000, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 0 (2^24)" },
173:		    {    50000000, 0xFFFFFF, TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256, "cats, slowest legal" },
174:		};
175:		const int ceiling = (int)(TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * (int)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
176:		size_t c;
177:		for (c = 0; c < sizeof(cases)/sizeof(cases[0]); c++) {
178:			int worst_peak = INT_MIN, worst_trough = INT_MAX, best_peak = INT_MAX;
179:			d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
180:			fake_machine.emulated_hz = cases[c].hz;
181:			for (nr = 0; nr < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; nr++) {
182:				arm(d, nr, cases[c].load, cases[c].ctrl);
183:				drive(d, nr, (long long)ceiling + 4096, &peak, &trough);
184:				if (peak > worst_peak)
185:					worst_peak = peak;
186:				if (peak < best_peak)
187:					best_peak = peak;
188:				if (trough < worst_trough)
189:					worst_trough = trough;
190:			}
191:			f = (double)cases[c].hz /
192:			    (double)footbridge_effective_cycles(cases[c].load & TIMER_MAX_VAL,
193:			    cases[c].ctrl, 1);
194:			row("R1 bounded across all 4 timers",
195:			    worst_peak <= ceiling && worst_trough >= 0,
196:			    "%-28s rate %12.2f Hz  peak %11d trough %11d  (ceiling %d)",
197:			    cases[c].what, f, worst_peak, worst_trough, ceiling);
198:
199:			/*
200:			 *  R5 -- THE BOUND IS THE TIMER'S OWN RATE, AS AN EQUALITY.
201:			 *
202:			 *  *** R1 ABOVE IS AN INEQUALITY AGAINST A CONSTANT, AND A
203:			 *  PASS-2 SEAT MEASURED FIVE MUTANTS WALKING THROUGH IT. ***  It
204:			 *  computes `f` -- the case's own delivered rate -- PRINTS it,
205:			 *  and then compares nothing to it, so ANY bound between R0's
206:			 *  floor and the ceiling satisfies it.
207:			 *
208:			 *  The one that matters is `double bound = freq / 100.0;` -- ONE
209:			 *  TOKEN.  Measured at rung 3 with a 0.5 s accrual: the shipped
210:			 *  build owes the guest 375 interrupts and that mutant delivers
211:			 *  FOUR.  It destroys 99% of the guest's clock and BOTH detectors
212:			 *  said PASS.  That is exactly the failure timer.h's "THE BACKLOG
213:			 *  IS RETAINED" paragraph exists to prevent.
214:			 *
215:			 *  An equality closes it, needs no new oracle, and costs nothing:
216:			 *  `f` was already computed for the message above.
217:			 *
218:			 *  *** #548: ON EVERY TIMER, NOT THE MAXIMUM.  A store lowering timers
219:			 *  2-3 alone (`(int) bound & ~(timer_nr << 16)`) hid behind timer 0's
220:			 *  correct peak at every case here.  The lowest peak must match too. ***
221:			 */
222:			{
223:				int want = (int) f;
224:				if (want < 1)
225:					want = 1;
226:				if (want > ceiling)
227:					want = ceiling;
228:				row("R5 the bound IS this timer's rate",
229:				    worst_peak == want && best_peak == want,
230:				    "%-28s peak %11d  want %11d  lowest %d", cases[c].what,
231:				    worst_peak, want, best_peak);
232:			}
233:			/*
234:			 *  R3 -- ONE INTERRUPT IS STILL OWED, and it is a separate row
235:			 *  from R1 because a bound that resets to ZERO satisfies R1
236:			 *  perfectly while dropping an interrupt the guest was owed.
237:			 *  luna88k resets to 1 for exactly this reason
238:			 *  (dev_luna88k.c:255-257: "restart the nr of pending
239:			 *  interrupts", not "forget them").  Measured: this row is the
240:			 *  only one of the four that separates reset-to-1 from
241:			 *  reset-to-0, and the rung-3 guest probe sees it only when
242:			 *  the delivered count is a multiple of 763, as a dead clock
243:			 *  (#548; this said "cannot see the difference at all").
244:			 */
245:			row("R3 the reset still owes one interrupt",
246:			    worst_trough >= 1,
247:			    "%-28s lowest value the counter ever held: %d",
248:			    cases[c].what, worst_trough);
249:			free(d);
250:		}
251:	}
252:
253:	/*
254:	 *  R2 -- NO SIGNED OVERFLOW, stated as its own row because R1's ceiling is a
255:	 *  CHOICE and this one is not.  2^31 + 4096 increments at the worst-case rate;
256:	 *  the counter must never be negative.  Skipped unless a depth is given, because
257:	 *  it costs a few seconds -- but the default depth is still above any cap the
258:	 *  fix could reasonably choose.
259:	 */
260:	if (deep > 0) {
261:		d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
262:		fake_machine.emulated_hz = 2147483647;
263:		arm(d, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);
264:		drive(d, 0, deep, &peak, &trough);
265:		row("R2 no signed overflow", trough >= 0 && peak >= 0,
266:		    "%lld direct ticks: peak %d trough %d", deep, peak, trough);
267:		free(d);
268:	} else {
269:		printf("  --   R2 no signed overflow                       "
270:		       "SKIPPED (pass a depth, e.g. 2147487744)\n");
271:	}
272:
273:	/*
274:	 *  R6 -- THE BOUND IS RECOMPUTED ON RE-ARM, AND IT IS PER TIMER.
275:	 *
276:	 *  *** THE CASE LOOP ABOVE ARMS ALL FOUR TIMERS WITH IDENTICAL PARAMETERS AND
277:	 *  ARMS EACH EXACTLY ONCE, so it is structurally blind to two mutants a pass-2
278:	 *  seat measured walking through everything: *** a bound FROZEN after the first
279:	 *  arm (recomputation stops mattering) and a bound written to `[0]` instead of
280:	 *  `[timer_nr]` (every timer shares timer 0's).  Neither can be seen by a table
281:	 *  where all four timers always agree and nothing is ever re-armed.
282:	 *
283:	 *  So: RE-ARM each timer at a different rate and require its bound to track
284:	 *  the new one (R6), and give every timer a rate no other timer shares (R6b).
285:	 *  Same oracle as R5 -- the peak the counter reaches -- plus the count the
286:	 *  timer is LEFT with, so no new oracle is introduced.
287:	 *
288:	 *  *** #548: A RE-ARM HAS TWO CELLS, AND EVERY TIMER OWES BOTH. ***  The
289:	 *  callback's `>=` splits a re-arm by where the count sits against the NEW
290:	 *  bound: below it (re-armed at count 0) or at or past it (re-armed owing 8192).
291:	 *  `==` for `>=` is identical in the first cell and, while nothing acks, never
292:	 *  fires again in the second; it passed every row in the battery, and one guest
293:	 *  CONTROL write left it 4529 owed against a bound of 762 (measured).  And `nr`
294:	 *  indexes both lvalues the guard reads, so the timer is a floor axis too: a
295:	 *  first repair that built the owed cell on timer 2 alone let `==` on the other
296:	 *  three timers pass everything, 4529 owed again at rung 3 (measured).  So all
297:	 *  EIGHT cells, each timer re-armed to its OWN rate (762/1525/3051/6103), so a
298:	 *  re-arm that stores into another timer's slot shows as well.  The END counts --
299:	 *  ((8192-1) mod B)+1 under the bound -- tie each peak to its own timer.
300:	 *
301:	 *  THE CONTRACT: covered, every timer x {re-armed at count 0, re-armed owing
302:	 *  8192}, downward re-arms from 50 MHz.  NOT rowed, and filed (fbbounddebt): a
303:	 *  STORE conditioned by a mutant on the count (`pending != (int)bound` at a
304:	 *  count of exactly B, `pending == 0 || pending >= (int)bound` at 1..B-1) or
305:	 *  on the direction (a store skipped on an UPWARD re-arm), and an index read
306:	 *  from another field (`timer_load[nr]`, `timer_control[nr]`).  The fix's own
307:	 *  store is unconditioned and its index is `timer_nr` alone; these cells are
308:	 *  not built for proportion, not because a rule excuses them.
309:	 */
310:	{
311:		static const uint32_t rl[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS] = { 256, 128, 64, 32 };
312:		int s, t, bad = 0, owe, want, owed[2][N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS],
313:		    pk[2][N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS], end[2][N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
314:		char line[640];
315:		size_t o = 0;
316:
317:		/*  Struct 0 re-arms timers 0-1 at count 0 and timers 2-3 owing 8192;
318:		    struct 1 swaps the roles.  */
319:		fake_machine.emulated_hz = 50000000;
320:		for (s = 0; s < 2; s++) {
321:			struct footbridge_data *r6 =
322:			    (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*r6));
323:			for (t = 0; t < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; t++) {
324:				arm(r6, t, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);		/*  50 MHz  */
325:				if (((t >> 1) ^ s) & 1)
326:					drive(r6, t, 8192, &peak, &trough);
327:			}
328:			for (t = 0; t < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; t++) {
329:				owe = (((t >> 1) ^ s) & 1) ? 8192 : 0;
330:				want = (int) (50000000.0 / (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(
331:				    rl[t], TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256, 1));
332:				owed[s][t] = r6->pending_timer_interrupts[t];
333:				arm(r6, t, rl[t], TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256);
334:				drive(r6, t, 8192, &pk[s][t], &trough);
335:				end[s][t] = r6->pending_timer_interrupts[t];
336:				if (owed[s][t] != owe || pk[s][t] != want ||
337:				    end[s][t] < 1 || end[s][t] > want)
338:					bad++;
339:			}
340:			free(r6);
341:		}
342:		for (s = 0; s < 2; s++)
343:			for (t = 0; t < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; t++)
344:				o += (size_t) snprintf(line + o, sizeof(line) - o, "%s%d/%d/%d",
345:				    (s || t) ? " " : "", owed[s][t], pk[s][t], end[s][t]);
346:		row("R6 a re-armed timer's bound follows its NEW rate", bad == 0,
347:		    "owed/peak/end, 4 timers x 2 structs: %s", line);
348:	}
349:
350:	/*
351:	 *  R6b -- EVERY TIMER CARRIES ITS OWN BOUND, AT A RATE NO OTHER TIMER SHARES.
352:	 *
353:	 *  *** #548: THIS ROW CHECKED TIMER 1 AT 762 Hz -- THE RATE TIMER 0 HAD ALSO
354:	 *  ENDED AT -- SO A TIMER READING ANOTHER TIMER'S BOUND READ THE SAME NUMBER AND
355:	 *  PASSED. ***  Measured: the compare reading `pending_bound[0]` for every timer
356:	 *  passed every row in the battery.  At four DISTINCT rates a timer that stores
357:	 *  into, or compares against, another timer's slot -- or an unset one -- reads a
358:	 *  different peak.  All four are armed FIRST, so a store that overwrites another
359:	 *  timer's slot shows too.  Index maps of `nr` alone are what this closes; an
360:	 *  index computed from another field is outside it (fbbounddebt, above).
361:	 */
362:	{
363:		static const uint32_t ld[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS] = { 256, 128, 64, 32 };
364:		int pk[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS], want[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS], trough, t, bad = 0;
365:		struct footbridge_data *r6b =
366:		    (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*r6b));
367:
368:		fake_machine.emulated_hz = 50000000;
369:		for (t = 0; t < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; t++)
370:			arm(r6b, t, ld[t], TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256);
371:		for (t = 0; t < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; t++) {
372:			want[t] = (int) (50000000.0 / (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(ld[t],
373:			    TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256, 1));
374:			drive(r6b, t, 8192, &pk[t], &trough);
375:			if (pk[t] != want[t])
376:				bad++;
377:		}
378:		row("R6b each timer carries its OWN bound", bad == 0,
379:		    "distinct rates: peaks %d %d %d %d want %d %d %d %d", pk[0], pk[1],
380:		    pk[2], pk[3], want[0], want[1], want[2], want[3]);
381:		free(r6b);
382:	}
383:
384:	/*
385:	 *  @@SELFCHECK@@ -- the SECOND failability control, asking a different question
386:	 *  from the self-mutant in gate_offline.sh.  That one breaks dev_footbridge.c and
387:	 *  demands a NAMED row notice, so it catches a fixture that has stopped reaching
388:	 *  the code.  This one breaks nothing and feeds row() itself a deliberate
389:	 *  mismatch, so it catches a comparator edited into always-passing -- a state in
390:	 *  which every row prints `ok` and the row count AND the verdict token both
391:	 *  survive.  Neither catches the other's failure.
392:	 *
393:	 *  The token in the row name is load-bearing: selfmutant.py filters exactly it,
394:	 *  and without it these deliberate FAIL lines would be read as a mutant kill for
395:	 *  a mutant nothing caught.
396:	 */
397:	{
398:		int sc_f = failures, sc_r = rows, sc_bad = 0;
399:
400:		row("@@SELFCHECK@@/false", 0, "a deliberate mismatch");
401:		if (failures <= sc_f)
402:			sc_bad++;
403:		failures = sc_f; rows = sc_r;
404:
405:		row("@@SELFCHECK@@/true", 1, "a deliberate match");
406:		if (failures != sc_f)
407:			sc_bad++;
408:		failures = sc_f; rows = sc_r;
409:
410:		/*  sc_bad is a SEPARATE local that survives the restore above -- restoring
411:		    `failures` is what makes the sentinel non-destructive, so the evidence
412:		    cannot live in `failures` itself.  */
413:		row("SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail", sc_bad == 0,
414:		    "both directions");
415:	}
416:
417:	/*
418:	 *  R4 -- THE AGGREGATE: red whenever any case above is.  #442 added it as the
419:	 *  self-mutant lane's pin, believing R1/R3 -- printed once per emulated_hz/load
420:	 *  case -- could not be pinned.  They can: selfmutant.py matches the pin anywhere
421:	 *  in the printed line, so one case's R3 row is unique.  And an aggregate pin is
422:	 *  VERDICT-EQUIVALENT, so #537 pins R3's "cats, slowest legal" row instead;
423:	 *  R4 stays as the every-case summary.
424:	 */
425:	row("R4 every case bounded", failures == 0,
426:	    "%d row failure(s) above", failures);
427:
428:	/*
429:	 *  IDENTITY -- the row count asserts itself.  Without it, DELETING a row is
430:	 *  invisible: every remaining row prints ok, the verdict token survives, and the
431:	 *  gate's `rows actually run` floor only notices a drop BELOW the floor.  This is
432:	 *  the constblind shape, and gate section E hard-fails a new differential lacking
433:	 *  it.
434:	 *
435:	 *  `rows + 1` counts this row itself, which has not been printed yet -- arguments
436:	 *  are evaluated before the call.  The expectation is CONDITIONAL on `deep`,
437:	 *  because R2 only runs when a depth is given, and hardcoding one value would make
438:	 *  a no-depth run red for no defect.  The SELFCHECK block restores `rows`, so its
439:	 *  two deliberate rows do not count while its verdict row does.
440:	 */
441:	row("IDENTITY: row count", rows + 1 == (deep > 0 ? IDENT_DEEP : IDENT_SHALLOW),
442:	    "%d rows (expect %d with R2, %d without)",
443:	    rows + 1, IDENT_DEEP, IDENT_SHALLOW);
444:
445:	/*  #537: the house verdict token, `<WORD>_PASS' / `<WORD>_FAIL'.  This printed
446:	    `FBBOUND_VERDICT=PASS', which selfmutant.py's `\b([A-Z0-9_]+_PASS)\b' can never
447:	    match -- the one deviation that kept this harness's self-mutant lane out of gate 2
448:	    since #442 (diagnosed as `fbsmlane', 2026-08-27).  gate_offline.sh greps this line.  */
449:	printf("\nFBBOUND_%s\n", failures ? "FAIL" : "PASS");
450:	return failures ? 1 : 0;
451:}

127:		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;

142:/*
143: *  #432: ONE interpretation of the load register, used by BOTH consumers.
144: *
145: *  THE CRASH.  DEVICE_TICK computed `random() % timer_load[i]`, and TIMER_x_LOAD accepts a
146: *  guest-written zero (`idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL`), so a guest could divide by zero and take the
147: *  HOST process down -- reproduced as SIGFPE, exit 136, by two seats independently.
148: *
149: *  THERE ARE TWO ROUTES TO IT, and only one is ours.  Route 1: a zero load makes the rate
150: *  +INF, which before #427/#428 meant interval 0.0 and a catch-up loop that never advanced,
151: *  so the machine froze before this function ran; the clamp and the bound opened that path.
152: *  ROUTE 2 PREDATES BOTH: TIMER_x_LOAD does not clear pending_timer_interrupts (TIMER_x_CONTROL
153: *  below does), so an ORDINARY LEGAL timer accrues a backlog and a later write of zero divides
154: *  -- measured on the pre-#427 core, it crashes there too.  DEVICE_TICK runs from machine_run(),
155: *  the main loop, not from the signal handler, so the CPU reaches the divide between signals.
156: *
157: *  WHY A HELPER RATHER THAN TWO GUARDS.  Three seats converged on this independently, and the
158: *  measuring seat REFUTED each single-site alternative: guarding only this function still
159: *  SIGFPEs by route 2 (and is the most dangerous option precisely because it passes the obvious
160: *  test); guarding only the modulo leaves the backlog growing 1048576 per signal, reaching
161: *  INT_MAX in ~31 s; and normalising at the guest write corrupts read-back -- TIMER_x_LOAD
162: *  returns the stored value, so the guest would read something it never wrote, which invents an
163: *  answer to a question the hardware documentation we lack would have to settle.
164: *
165: *  THE REGISTER STAYS RAW.  timer_load[] keeps exactly what the guest wrote, masked to 24 bits.
166: *  Only the DERIVED quantities are interpreted here, and they are model-internal.
167: *
168: *  ZERO MEANS FULL PERIOD (2^24), and it is a CHOICE UNDER A DOCUMENTED UNKNOWN, not a fact.
169: *
170: *  The 21285 datasheet was obtained for this round (_scratchpad/dc21285.pdf, Intel order
171: *  278115-001, Sept 1998).  SS 7.3.39 (p. 7-50) specifies TimerNLoad and its reset value without
172: *  saying what a count of zero does, and a search of the full extraction for "minimum count" /
173: *  "nonzero" / "reaches zero" turns up no equivalent of the 8254's Figure 22.  So the absence is
174: *  MEASURED, not assumed -- a stronger thing to say than the guess it replaces.
175: *
176: *  BUT "SILENT" OVERSTATES IT BY HALF, and the pass-2 review caught that in this round's favour.
177: *  SS 7.3.41 bit 6 says a FREE-RUNNING timer "will wrap to FFFFFFh and continue to decrement",
178: *  which together with SS 6.4's 24-bit down counter DETERMINES the free-run case: a load of zero
179: *  gives a period of exactly 2^24, i.e. the mapping below is datasheet-IMPLIED there -- and
180: *  free-running is bit 6 == 0, the reset mode.  It is genuinely unknown only for PERIODIC.
181: *
182: *  The choice therefore rests on failure asymmetry, and there are THREE candidate truths rather
183: *  than the two first written here.  Implement full-period when the truth is "disabled" and a
184: *  guest gets a few unwanted interrupts on a timer it explicitly enabled -- noisy, visible,
185: *  recoverable.  Implement "disabled" when the truth is full-period and the guest waits forever
186: *  on a tick that never comes -- silent, and it hangs at boot.  And the third, which the first
187: *  draft omitted: read mechanically, PERIODIC mode with a zero load reloads zero and interrupts
188: *  every prescaled clock -- a max-rate storm, the DANGEROUS branch rather than the silent one.
189: *  Full-period is the only choice that survives all three, so the argument was understating its
190: *  own robustness.
191: *
192: *  A SECOND SUPPORT WAS ALSO WITHDRAWN, for the same reason as the third and by a seat whose
193: *  answer sat unread for a day.  It said the 24-bit mask aliases "a legitimate guest write of
194: *  0x1000000 -- one full wrap -- to zero, so this mapping keeps the rate continuous across the
195: *  register's own truncation".  The datasheet closes it: SS 7.3.39 gives TimerNLoad as bits 23:0
196: *  R/W with "31:24 -- R Read only as 0", so bit 24 is not a truncated write at all, it is OUTSIDE
197: *  THE WRITABLE FIELD.  There is no wrap to be continuous across, and continuity beyond a
198: *  register's own width is a modelling preference dressed up as arithmetic.  Zero -> 2^24 stands
199: *  on the three-way argument above and on nothing else; what silicon does with a zero load
200: *  remains UNKNOWN, and SS 7.3.39 is silent on it.
201: *
202: *  A THIRD ARGUMENT WAS WITHDRAWN WHEN THE DATASHEET ARRIVED, and it is recorded because the
203: *  withdrawal matters more than the argument did.  An earlier draft of this comment said
204: *  "devinit resets every load to TIMER_MAX_VAL, so the model's own convention is full width".
205: *  The datasheet says TimerNLoad and TimerNControl BOTH reset to 0 (SS 7.3.39, SS 7.3.41), so
206: *  this model's reset values are themselves wrong -- that line was citing a model bug as
207: *  evidence.  The divergence is filed; it is not fixed here.
208: *
209: *  THE WIDTH FIX IS NOT SEPARABLE FROM THE ZERO FIX.  The old `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`
210: *  overflowed for exactly half the legal 24-bit range (first bad load 0x800000), yielding a
211: *  NEGATIVE frequency that the core's floor clamp silently turned into one tick per ~3.2 years
212: *  -- reachable with NO load write at all, since the reset load is TIMER_MAX_VAL and a single
213: *  write of ENABLE|FCLK_256 gets there.  And mapping zero to 2^24 while leaving `int` gives
214: *  2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0, which is +INF AGAIN: the obvious zero-guard RE-CREATES the bug it
215: *  removes.  Hence uint64_t, and multiplication rather than a signed shift.  Found independently
216: *  by three seats.
217: *
218: *  (8253 maps a zero divisor to "disabled" instead -- and a pass-2 seat caught that an earlier
219: *  version of this parenthetical argued the point BACKWARDS.  It said the 8253's 16-bit counter
220: *  "has no equivalent of the 24-bit aliasing above", but writing 0x10000 to a 16-bit latch aliases
221: *  to zero in exactly the same way -- which is precisely WHY the Intel 8254 datasheet defines a
222: *  programmed count of 0 as 2^16 (231164-005, p. 17).  So the aliasing argument REINFORCES this
223: *  mapping rather than distinguishing it, and dev_8253.c's "disabled" is the one that looks wrong
224: *  against its own hardware.  Filed as its own item; not changed here.)
225: */
226:/*
227: *  with_prescaler distinguishes the two consumers, and it is NOT a convenience flag -- getting
228: *  it wrong is a defect the measuring seat caught in its own first proposal.  The prescaler
229: *  slows the RATE; it does not widen the COUNTER.  Feeding the prescaled period to the modulo
230: *  put values up to 0x7fffca11 into a 24-bit register that the guest can read back -- measured,
231: *  19833 of 20000 ticks out of range.  So: the rate consumer prescales, the counter consumer
232: *  does not, and both get their never-zero span from this one function.
233: */
234:static uint64_t footbridge_effective_cycles(uint32_t load, uint32_t control,
235:	int with_prescaler)
236:{
237:	uint64_t cycles = load;
238:
239:	if (cycles == 0)
240:		cycles = (uint64_t)TIMER_MAX_VAL + 1;	/*  0x1000000 == 2^24  */
241:
242:	if (!with_prescaler)
243:		return cycles;
244:
245:	/*  The prescaler scales the PERIOD.  It must not be a signed shift, and the order
246:	    matters: TIMER_EXTERNAL is FCLK_16|FCLK_256, so test the pair first or 0x0C reads
247:	    as a bare FCLK_16 by luck of the `else if`.  */
248:	if ((control & (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256))
249:	    == (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256)) {
250:		/*  TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C): a LEGAL encoding, and this is now DATASHEET-BACKED
251:		    rather than inferred from the header.  The 21285 datasheet obtained for this
252:		    round gives TimerNControl bits 3:2 as 00=fclk_in, 01=fclk_in/16,
253:		    10=fclk_in/256, 11=External event (SS 7.3.41, p. 7-51; SS 6.4, p. 6-11 lists
254:		    the same four).  It used to reach fatal()+exit(1) below, so a CONFORMING guest
255:		    selecting the external clock source killed the host -- the same defect class as
256:		    the divide and arguably likelier, being a documented mode rather than a
257:		    malformed value.  The RATE an external source would give depends on a pin this
258:		    model has no signal for (irq_in_l), so it is genuinely not derivable here and
259:		    is NOT invented: the unprescaled period keeps the timer sane and the emulator
260:		    alive.  Modelling the external event properly is filed.  */
261:		;
262:	} else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_16)
263:		cycles *= 16;
264:	else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_256)
265:		cycles *= 256;
266:
267:	return cycles;
268:}
269:
270:
271:static void reload_timer_value(struct cpu *cpu, struct footbridge_data *d,
272:	int timer_nr)
273:{
274:	double freq = (double)cpu->machine->emulated_hz;
275:
276:	freq /= (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[timer_nr],
277:	    d->timer_control[timer_nr], 1);
278:
279:	d->timer_value[timer_nr] = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
280:
281:	/*
282:	 *  #442: one emulated second of THIS timer's delivered rate, clamped.
283:	 *
284:	 *  The ceiling is DERIVED, not chosen: TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP ticks per timer
285:	 *  per signal at TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY signals per second is the most the
286:	 *  timer core can ever deliver to one timer in one wall second, so a cap
287:	 *  at that value can never throttle a legitimately fast timer.
288:	 *
289:	 *  IT IS ALSO NOT OPTIONAL, which a purely rate-relative bound hides.
290:	 *  With `-I 2147483647`, load 1 and no prescaler the requested rate is
291:	 *  2147483647.0, so (int)freq is INT_MAX: a debt of 2^31-1 ticks, 31.5 s
292:	 *  at the most the core delivers -- NOT an overflow, as this said until
293:	 *  #548: the compare precedes the ++ (R2 measured green at INT_MAX).
294:	 *  Deleting these two lines passes the rung-3 probe; R1/R5 catch it.
295:	 *
296:	 *  Floor 1.  *** ITS ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS MEASURED FALSE and is
297:	 *  corrected here rather than quietly dropped: *** the comment claimed a
298:	 *  0.0116 Hz timer "would otherwise bound at 0 and the counter would be
299:	 *  pinned there".  With the floor PRESENT that case already reads peak 1,
300:	 *  and with the floor DELETED the harness output is BYTE-IDENTICAL -- a
301:	 *  bound of 0 and a bound of 1 both pin at 1.  The floor prevents nothing
302:	 *  today; it is kept as a guard against a future bound expression that
303:	 *  could go negative, and that is the honest reason.
304:	 *
305:	 *  The comparisons are negated, but NOT for the reason first written.
306:	 *  timer_clamp_freq() puts its FLOOR first deliberately (timer.c:80-82:
307:	 *  "the FLOOR is tested FIRST so a NaN lands SLOW rather than fast"), and
308:	 *  this tests the CEILING first -- the inverse.  Harmless, because NaN is
309:	 *  UNREACHABLE here: emulated_hz is an int (machine.h:191) and
310:	 *  footbridge_effective_cycles() is >= 1 by construction, so this is
311:	 *  finite / finite-nonzero.  The claim to "match timer_clamp_freq's idiom"
312:	 *  was false in the one respect that idiom is about.
313:	 *
314:	 *  Set before timer_add()/timer_update_frequency() below.  *** THE REASON
315:	 *  FIRST GIVEN HERE -- that a SIGALRM landing between them would find a
316:	 *  zero bound -- IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, measured. ***  On the FIRST arm the
317:	 *  timer is not in first_timer until timer_add() links it, and timer.c
318:	 *  publishes fully-initialised (next stored before first_timer); on later
319:	 *  arms the OLD bound is still a valid bound.  A mutant moving this store
320:	 *  below the switch is a ZERO-BYTE object delta and passes every row.
321:	 *
322:	 *  Kept in this order because it reads correctly and costs nothing, not
323:	 *  because it closes a window.  Note also that this and the DEVINIT seed
324:	 *  are REDUNDANT WITH EACH OTHER -- either alone removes the zero-bound
325:	 *  state -- and neither comment used to mention the other doing the work.
326:	 */
327:	{
328:		double bound = freq;
329:		if (!(bound <= (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY))
330:			bound = (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY;
331:		if (!(bound >= 1.0))
332:			bound = 1.0;
333:		d->pending_bound[timer_nr] = (int) bound;
334:	}
335:
336:	/*  printf("%i: %i -> %f Hz\n", timer_nr,
337:	    d->timer_load[timer_nr], freq);  */
338:
339:	if (d->timer[timer_nr] == NULL) {
340:		switch (timer_nr) {
600:		} else {
601:			/*  #539: a read of 188h is documented -- IRQEnable is
602:			    "read-only ... can only be changed by writing to
603:			    the EnableSet and EnableClear registers", reset 0
604:			    (DC21285 Table 7-10, 7.3.31).  The value below was
605:			    always right; the exit(1) that followed it is gone.  */
606:			odata = d->irq_enable;
607:		}
608:		break;
609:
610:	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
611:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
612:			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
613:			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
614:				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
615:			else
616:				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
617:		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
618:			/*  #545: IRQEnableClear is write-only (7.3.33) and Table
619:			    7-10 names no register at 18Ch for reads, so the read
620:			    is declined with 0 -- the IRQEnable value that used to
621:			    be computed here is 188h's answer, not 18Ch's.  */
622:			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
623:			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
624:			    "ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]\n");
625:		}
626:		break;
627:
628:	case FIQ_STATUS:
629:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
630:			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
631:		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
632:			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.29); declined, announced once.  */
633:			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
634:			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
635:			    "(once) ]\n");
636:		}
637:		break;
638:
639:	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
640:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
641:			odata = d->fiq_status;
642:		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
643:			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
644:			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
645:			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
646:		}
647:		break;
648:
649:	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
650:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
651:			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
652:		break;
653:
654:	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
655:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
656:			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
657:		break;
658:
659:	case TIMER_1_LOAD:
660:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
661:			odata = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
662:		else {
663:			d->timer_load[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
664:			reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
665:			/*  debug("[ footbridge: timer %i (1-based), "
666:			    "value %i ]\n", timer_nr + 1,
667:			    (int)d->timer_value[timer_nr]);  */
668:			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
669:		}
670:		break;
671:
672:	case TIMER_1_VALUE:
673:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
674:			odata = d->timer_value[timer_nr];
675:		else
676:			d->timer_value[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
677:		break;
678:
679:	case TIMER_1_CONTROL:
680:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
681:			odata = d->timer_control[timer_nr];
682:		else {
683:			d->timer_control[timer_nr] = idata;
684:			/*  #432: both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL
685:			    (0x0C, dc21285reg.h:364) -- a LEGAL named encoding,
686:			    and it used to reach fatal()+exit(1) right here, so a
687:			    guest selecting the external clock source KILLED THE
688:			    HOST.  Arguably likelier than the zero load, since it
689:			    is a documented mode rather than a malformed value.
690:			    The rate an external clock would actually give is
691:			    UNKNOWN and is NOT invented: footbridge_effective_cycles()
692:			    now gives the encoding a defined, survivable meaning
693:			    (the unprescaled period), and the real semantics are
694:			    filed for a round that has a datasheet.  */
695:			if (idata & TIMER_ENABLE) {
696:				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
697:			} else {
698:				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0;
699:			}
700:			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
701:		}
702:		break;
703:
704:	case TIMER_1_CLEAR:
705:		/*  #474: a READ must not perform the clear.  This case carried no
706:		    writeflag test at all, while TIMER_1_CONTROL immediately above it
707:		    does -- so a guest READ reloaded the counter, decremented the
708:		    pending count and DEASSERTED the interrupt.  A guest that merely
709:		    read the register silently acknowledged a pending timer interrupt;
710:		    the guest-visible consequence is a LOST interrupt, not a bad value.
711:
712:		    FOUR addresses, not one: the fold above normalises 0x30C/0x32C/
713:		    0x34C/0x36C onto this single case.
714:
715:		    GUARD THE BODY, KEEP THE break.  An early return here would skip
716:		    the function's common tail, which writes odata back on a read, and
717:		    would hand a guest an untouched buffer -- a defect this code does
718:		    not currently have.  */
719:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
720:			if (d->timer_control[timer_nr] & TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC) {
721:				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
722:			}
723:
724:			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] > 0) {
725:				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] --;
726:			}
727:
728:			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
729:		}
730:		break;
731:
732:	case SDRAM_BA_MASK:
733:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
734:			fatal("[ footbridge read from sdram_ba_mask ]\n");
735:			exit(1);
736:		} else {
737:			switch (idata) {
738:			case SDRAM_MASK_256KB:
739:			case SDRAM_MASK_512KB:
740:			case SDRAM_MASK_1MB:

 succeeded in 184ms:
105: *  THE COMPARISON PRECEDES THE INCREMENT, and that is not a style choice.  An
106: *  increment-then-check form -- which is what the one in-tree precedent for this
107: *  (dev_luna88k.c:252) actually does -- reintroduces the UB it is meant to remove
108: *  if the counter ever reaches INT_MAX, because the ++ has already executed by the
109: *  time the check runs.  luna88k is safe only because its own reset keeps the
110: *  counter far below INT_MAX; the FORM is still the wrong one to copy.
111: *
112: *  Reset to 1 rather than 0, so one interrupt is still owed -- coalescing the debt
113: *  rather than discarding it.  MEASURED to matter: a reset-to-0 mutant survives the
114: *  rung-3 drain probe and two of the three offline rows; only the trough row
115: *  separates them (0 against 1).
116: *
117: *  NO MESSAGE FROM HERE.  These run in the SIGALRM handler and debugmsg() is
118: *  printf underneath, so copying luna88k's diagnostic would put four printf paths
119: *  inside a signal handler -- and timer.c:274-279 states that nothing in that file
120: *  prints from the handler.  (luna88k's own callback does, which is a defect there;
121: *  filed, not fixed here.)  If a diagnostic is ever wanted, latch a flag here and
122: *  report from DEVICE_TICK, which runs in the main loop.
123: */
124:static void footbridge_pending(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr)
125:{
126:	if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] >= d->pending_bound[nr])
127:		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;
128:	else
129:		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] ++;
130:}
131:
132:static void timer_tick0(struct timer *t, void *extra)
133:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 0); }
134:static void timer_tick1(struct timer *t, void *extra)
135:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 1); }
136:static void timer_tick2(struct timer *t, void *extra)
137:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 2); }
138:static void timer_tick3(struct timer *t, void *extra)
139:{ footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 3); }

271:static void reload_timer_value(struct cpu *cpu, struct footbridge_data *d,
272:	int timer_nr)
273:{
274:	double freq = (double)cpu->machine->emulated_hz;
275:
276:	freq /= (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[timer_nr],
277:	    d->timer_control[timer_nr], 1);

282:	 *  #442: one emulated second of THIS timer's delivered rate, clamped.
283:	 *

285:	 *  per signal at TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY signals per second is the most the
286:	 *  timer core can ever deliver to one timer in one wall second, so a cap
287:	 *  at that value can never throttle a legitimately fast timer.
288:	 *
289:	 *  IT IS ALSO NOT OPTIONAL, which a purely rate-relative bound hides.
290:	 *  With `-I 2147483647`, load 1 and no prescaler the requested rate is
291:	 *  2147483647.0, so (int)freq is INT_MAX: a debt of 2^31-1 ticks, 31.5 s
292:	 *  at the most the core delivers -- NOT an overflow, as this said until
293:	 *  #548: the compare precedes the ++ (R2 measured green at INT_MAX).
294:	 *  Deleting these two lines passes the rung-3 probe; R1/R5 catch it.
295:	 *
296:	 *  Floor 1.  *** ITS ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS MEASURED FALSE and is
297:	 *  corrected here rather than quietly dropped: *** the comment claimed a
298:	 *  0.0116 Hz timer "would otherwise bound at 0 and the counter would be
299:	 *  pinned there".  With the floor PRESENT that case already reads peak 1,
300:	 *  and with the floor DELETED the harness output is BYTE-IDENTICAL -- a
301:	 *  bound of 0 and a bound of 1 both pin at 1.  The floor prevents nothing
302:	 *  today; it is kept as a guard against a future bound expression that
303:	 *  could go negative, and that is the honest reason.
304:	 *
305:	 *  The comparisons are negated, but NOT for the reason first written.
306:	 *  timer_clamp_freq() puts its FLOOR first deliberately (timer.c:80-82:
307:	 *  "the FLOOR is tested FIRST so a NaN lands SLOW rather than fast"), and
308:	 *  this tests the CEILING first -- the inverse.  Harmless, because NaN is
309:	 *  UNREACHABLE here: emulated_hz is an int (machine.h:191) and
310:	 *  footbridge_effective_cycles() is >= 1 by construction, so this is
311:	 *  finite / finite-nonzero.  The claim to "match timer_clamp_freq's idiom"
312:	 *  was false in the one respect that idiom is about.
313:	 *
314:	 *  Set before timer_add()/timer_update_frequency() below.  *** THE REASON
315:	 *  FIRST GIVEN HERE -- that a SIGALRM landing between them would find a
316:	 *  zero bound -- IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, measured. ***  On the FIRST arm the
317:	 *  timer is not in first_timer until timer_add() links it, and timer.c
318:	 *  publishes fully-initialised (next stored before first_timer); on later
319:	 *  arms the OLD bound is still a valid bound.  A mutant moving this store
320:	 *  below the switch is a ZERO-BYTE object delta and passes every row.
321:	 *
322:	 *  Kept in this order because it reads correctly and costs nothing, not
323:	 *  because it closes a window.  Note also that this and the DEVINIT seed
324:	 *  are REDUNDANT WITH EACH OTHER -- either alone removes the zero-bound
325:	 *  state -- and neither comment used to mention the other doing the work.
625:		}
626:		break;
627:
628:	case FIQ_STATUS:
629:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
630:			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
631:		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
632:			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.29); declined, announced once.  */
633:			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
634:			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
635:			    "(once) ]\n");
636:		}
637:		break;
638:
639:	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
640:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
641:			odata = d->fiq_status;
642:		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
643:			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
644:			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
645:			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
646:		}
647:		break;
648:
649:	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
650:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
651:			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
652:		break;
653:
654:	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
655:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
656:			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
657:		break;
658:
659:	case TIMER_1_LOAD:
660:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
661:			odata = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
662:		else {
663:			d->timer_load[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
664:			reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
665:			/*  debug("[ footbridge: timer %i (1-based), "
666:			    "value %i ]\n", timer_nr + 1,
667:			    (int)d->timer_value[timer_nr]);  */
668:			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
669:		}
670:		break;
671:
672:	case TIMER_1_VALUE:
673:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
674:			odata = d->timer_value[timer_nr];
675:		else
676:			d->timer_value[timer_nr] = idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
677:		break;
678:
679:	case TIMER_1_CONTROL:
680:		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
681:			odata = d->timer_control[timer_nr];
682:		else {
683:			d->timer_control[timer_nr] = idata;
684:			/*  #432: both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL
685:			    (0x0C, dc21285reg.h:364) -- a LEGAL named encoding,
686:			    and it used to reach fatal()+exit(1) right here, so a
687:			    guest selecting the external clock source KILLED THE

 succeeded in 192ms:
1:#!/usr/bin/env python3
2:"""fbpending DRAIN PROBE -- a RUNG-3 detector that MEASURES the pending-interrupt
3:backlog of footbridge timer 1 in ~10 s, instead of waiting 43 s for it to overflow.
4:
5:WHY THIS EXISTS.  The rung-3 witness (fbpending_witness.py) proves the defect by
6:running until the counter wraps, which takes 42.95 s at cats' emulated_hz and cannot
7:be made faster than 31.51 s on ANY machine (the delivery ceiling is
8:TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP x TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY = 68,157,440 increments per wall second, and
9:timer_tick0 is called from nowhere else).  A gate row cannot spend that.
10:
11:THE TRICK: MEASURE THE COUNTER, DO NOT WAIT FOR IT TO OVERFLOW.
12:
13:  1. The guest arms timer 1 at a KNOWN SLOW rate.  cats' emulated_hz is 50,000,000 and
14:     reload_timer_value() asks for emulated_hz / effective_cycles(load, ctrl, 1).  With
15:     TIMER_FCLK_256 and load 256 that is 50e6 / (256*256) = 762.94 Hz.
16:  2. It free-runs for T seconds.  Nothing acks, so the backlog is rate*T -- UNLESS the
17:     device bounds it.
18:  3. It then FREEZES the timer by writing TIMER_1_LOAD = 0x00FFFFFF while keeping
19:     TIMER_FCLK_256: the rate falls to 50e6/(0xFFFFFF*256) = 0.0116 Hz, one tick per
20:     86 s.  *** A LOAD WRITE DOES NOT CLEAR pending_timer_interrupts *** (only a
21:     TIMER_x_CONTROL write WITHOUT TIMER_ENABLE does -- dev_footbridge.c, the
22:     `pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0` store in the `else` of `if (idata &
23:     TIMER_ENABLE)`), so the
24:     backlog survives the freeze.  That turns a moving quantity into a static one.
25:  4. It then DRAINS AND COUNTS.  DEVICE_TICK writes timer_value = random() %
26:     effective_cycles(load, ctrl, 0) whenever the backlog is > 0, and with load
27:     0x00FFFFFF that modulus is 0xFFFFFF, so the produced value is in [0, 0xFFFFFE].
28:     The guest writes the sentinel 0x00FFFFFF -- which the modulo CANNOT produce and
29:     which survives TIMER_x_VALUE's own `idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL` mask -- and spins.  A
30:     changed read means one tick was delivered; the guest then writes TIMER_1_CLEAR
31:     (one decrement) and repeats.  When the spin budget expires with the sentinel
32:     intact, the backlog is 0 and the number of decrements IS the backlog.
33:
34:  The measured number is the whole verdict: pre-fix it is rate*T.  No wrap, no 43 s,
35:  and the row reports a NUMBER rather than a boolean.
36:
37:  *** POST-FIX IT IS **NOT** THE CAP, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS PARAGRAPH SAID IT
38:  WAS. ***  Measured by a pass-2 seat on ONE binary with ONE bound of 762: backlog 3 at
39:  4 s, 100 at 46 s, 375 at 0.5 s.  Once the bound is first reached the number is a
40:  MODULAR RESIDUE of the delivered tick count, not the bound itself.
41:
42:  That is why `--expect-cap` is a one-sided `bD <= CAP` and CANNOT distinguish a bound
43:  of 762 from one of 7: the quantity it tests is structurally <= the bound whatever the
44:  bound is.  The rows that actually pin the bound's VALUE live in
45:  regress/diff_fbpending_bound.c (R5, an equality against the timer's own rate, and R6,
46:  which re-arms at a different rate) -- and they exist because a one-token mutant
47:  (`freq / 100.0`) destroyed 99% of the guest's clock while passing this probe.
48:
49:ARM X -- the reason it is not merely a second run.
50:  dev_footbridge.c never calls timer_remove() and never lowers the core frequency when
51:  the guest DISABLES a timer: TIMER_x_CONTROL without TIMER_ENABLE zeroes the counter
52:  once (the same `= 0` store) and leaves the struct timer running.  So timer_tickN keeps incrementing a
53:  counter that DEVICE_TICK will never look at.  Arm X arms the timer, immediately
54:  disables it, free-runs, re-enables (which does NOT clear the counter) and then
55:  measures.  #442 measured 3051 on the PRE-FIX build, identical to arm D -- which alone
56:  cannot show the disable executed.  #548's M_en did: with the bound gated on ENABLE,
57:  X read 3051 against D's 3.  No row re-checks it per run: FILED as `fbdrainxdisable`.
58:  *** #548: THE HEADING SAID "THE PLACEMENT DISCRIMINATOR" AND X IS NOT ONE -- MEASURED.
59:  A bound moved into DEVICE_TICK reads X=1 and PASSES: the FREEZE's LOAD write sets the
60:  bound to 1, and DEVICE_TICK clamps the count before the drain counts it.  gate 2
61:  (diff_fbpending_bound.c R1/R2/R5/R6) catches that placement.  What X DOES catch is a
62:  bound gated on a control bit its CTRL=0 write clears: TIMER_ENABLE, which
63:  diff_fbpending_bound.c -- every arm of which is enabled -- cannot see, and
64:  TIMER_FCLK_256, which that file's unprescaled cases catch too.  X is the only GATED
65:  rung-3 witness of either. ***
66:
67:ARMS
68:  D  enabled throughout.  Backlog after T seconds.
69:  X  disabled during the accrual.  Backlog after T seconds.  Q1's discriminator.
70:  T  negative control: MMU left on, so the footbridge is not mapped.  Must not return
71:     the device's answers.  (machine_cats.c's boot page tables map only the low 256 MB;
72:     the footbridge is at PA 0x42000000.)
73:  H1/H2 harness legs (#545) -- they REPLACED arm K, a read of IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (+0x18c) that
74:     the device used to answer with fatal() then exit(1); #545 declines it.  H1: an unknown -E
75:     name -- no prompt anywhere in the buffer, the diagnostic naming it, exit 1.  H2: arm D's prologue verified (21 steps, r3/r5/r6/r7),
76:     a fresh reg, quit, then one more reg that must fail with `dead` set BEFORE close(), exit 0.
77:     Every surviving arm must reap exit 0.  NOT COVERED: moving s.close() ahead of that next
78:     send keeps H2 green (the send fails on the closed descriptor), and the legs cannot defend
79:     their own predicates -- harness edits, reviewed, not rowed (footbridge_sites_probe.py).
80:
81:Every RAM output word is poisoned with 0xDEADBEEF first and every verdict row requires
82:the values it consults to be PRESENT.  Every STEPPED instruction is read back, and every
83:stepped NON-branch instruction is compared, as it EXECUTES, with the emulator's own
84:disassembly (the hand-assembled-encoding trap): the 21-word prologue, and arm D's drain
85:body (0x70-0xe4) in arm E, whose seven branch words carry no expected text and are read
86:back only.  *** #548: THIS SAID "EVERY PLANTED INSTRUCTION".  The accrual loop
87:(0x54-0x6c) and arm X's disable and re-enable words (0x54/0x58, 0x70/0x74) FREE-RUN
88:unchecked.  Filed as `fbdrainxdisable`. ***
89:
90:usage: fbpending_drain_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <four-byte-raw-stub>

100:def _opt(name, default, cast):
101:    if name in sys.argv:
102:        return cast(sys.argv[sys.argv.index(name) + 1])
103:    return default
104:
105:
106:ACCRUE_S = _opt("--accrue", 4.0, float)
107:EXPECT_CAP = _opt("--expect-cap", -1, int)   # -1 = report only
108:#  WHICH of the four timers.  dev_footbridge.c derives timer_nr as
109:#  (relative_addr >> 5) & 3, so timer N's registers sit at 0x300 + N*0x20 and
110:#  TIMER_1_* is INDEX 0.  A fix applied to only one of the four callbacks passes a
111:#  row that exercises only timer 1, which is why this is a parameter and not a
112:#  constant.
113:TIMER_NR = _opt("--timer", 0, int)
114:TBASE = 0x300 + 0x20 * TIMER_NR
115:T_LOAD, T_VALUE, T_CTRL, T_CLEAR = TBASE, TBASE + 4, TBASE + 8, TBASE + 12
116:
117:CODE = 0x00008000
118:DATA = 0x00009000
119:FB = 0x42000000
120:
121:EMU_HZ = 50000000.0
122:#  --fast drives the counter at the machine's FULL emulated_hz (load 1, no
123:#  prescaler = 50 MHz on cats), which is the only setting that reaches the 2^31
124:#  wrap inside a run: 2^31 / 50e6 = 42.95 s.  The default 762.94 Hz would need
125:#  32 days.  The freeze/drain machinery below is independent of the accrual rate.
126:FAST = "--fast" in sys.argv
127:LOAD_RUN = 0x001 if FAST else 0x100   # with FCLK_256 -> 50e6/65536 = 762.94 Hz
128:LOAD_FREEZE = 0x00FFFFFF              # with FCLK_256 -> 0.0116 Hz
129:CTRL_ON = 0x80 if FAST else 0x88      # TIMER_ENABLE [| TIMER_FCLK_256]
130:CTRL_OFF = 0x00
131:SENTINEL = 0x00FFFFFF                 # random() % 0xFFFFFF can never produce it
132:SPIN = 0x00010000                     # 65536 iterations ~ 20 device-tick periods
133:LIMIT = 200000
134:RATE = EMU_HZ / (LOAD_RUN * (1.0 if FAST else 256.0))
135:ONLY = _opt("--only", "", str)        # run a single arm, e.g. --only X
136:
137:VENDOR = 0x1011
138:LOADBACK = 0x00345678
139:POISON = 0xDEADBEEF
140:
141:# ---- RAM map (offsets from DATA) -----------------------------------------
142:K_LIVE, K_SRC, K_L, K_FRZ, K_CON, K_SEN, K_SPIN, K_COFF = (
143:    0x00, 0x04, 0x08, 0x0c, 0x10, 0x14, 0x18, 0x1c)
144:O_DRAIN, O_ITER, O_LASTV, O_VEND, O_PHASE, K_LIM = (
145:    0x20, 0x24, 0x28, 0x2c, 0x30, 0x34)
146:
147:CONSTS = {K_LIVE: 0x11223344, K_SRC: 0x12345678, K_L: LOAD_RUN,
148:          K_FRZ: LOAD_FREEZE, K_CON: CTRL_ON, K_SEN: SENTINEL,
149:          K_SPIN: SPIN, K_COFF: CTRL_OFF, K_LIM: LIMIT}
150:OUTS = (O_DRAIN, O_ITER, O_LASTV, O_VEND, O_PHASE)
151:
152:R = dict(r0=0, r1=1, r2=2, r3=3, r4=4, r5=5, r6=6, r7=7, r8=8, r9=9,
153:         sl=10, fp=11, ip=12, sp=13, lr=14, pc=15)
