155:
156:# ---------------------------------------------------------------- assembler
157:#  Encodings are GENERATED, not typed.  Every generated word that is STEPPED is read
158:#  back, and each stepped non-branch word is compared with the emulator's own
159:#  disassembly as it executes (arm E of the witness found this class of error four
160:#  times in this project).  #548: this said EVERY generated word -- the accrual loop
161:#  and arm X's disable words free-run, and branch words carry no expected text.
162:def _imm12(v):
163:    for rot in range(16):
164:        c = ((v << (2 * rot)) | (v >> (32 - 2 * rot))) & 0xFFFFFFFF
165:        if c < 256:
166:            return (rot << 8) | c
167:    raise ValueError("0x%x is not an ARM immediate" % v)
168:
169:
170:def mov(rd, imm):
171:    return 0xE3A00000 | (R[rd] << 12) | _imm12(imm), "mov %s,#0x%x" % (rd, imm)
172:
173:
174:def movr(rd, rm):
175:    return 0xE1A00000 | (R[rd] << 12) | R[rm], "mov %s,%s" % (rd, rm)
176:
177:
178:def ldr(rd, rn, off):
179:    return 0xE5900000 | (R[rn] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12) | off, \
180:        "ldr %s,[%s,#%d]" % (rd, rn, off)
181:
182:
183:def st(rd, rn, off):
184:    return 0xE5800000 | (R[rn] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12) | off, \
185:        "str %s,[%s,#%d]" % (rd, rn, off)
186:
187:
188:def cmpr(rn, rm):
189:    return 0xE1500000 | (R[rn] << 16) | R[rm], "cmps %s,%s" % (rn, rm)
190:
191:
192:def subs1(rd):
193:    return 0xE2500001 | (R[rd] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12), "subs %s,%s,#1" % (rd, rd)
194:
195:
196:def add1(rd):
197:    return 0xE2800001 | (R[rd] << 16) | (R[rd] << 12), "add %s,%s,#1" % (rd, rd)
198:
199:
200:def br(cond, target, here):
201:    off = ((target - here - 8) >> 2) & 0xFFFFFF
202:    return (cond << 28) | 0x0A000000 | off, None
203:
204:
205:NOP = (0xE1A00000, "mov r0,r0")
206:
207:
208:def build(mmu_on=False, disable_during_accrual=False):
209:    """Returns [(offset, word, expected_disassembly_or_None, comment)]."""
210:    p = []
211:
212:    def emit(off, wt, comment=""):
213:        p.append((off, wt[0], wt[1], comment))
214:
215:    if mmu_on:
216:        for o in (0x00, 0x04, 0x08):
217:            emit(o, NOP, "arm T: MMU LEFT ON -- the negative control")
218:    else:
219:        p.append((0x00, 0xEE110F10, "mrc 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "read CP15 c1"))
220:        p.append((0x04, 0xE3C00001, "bic r0,r0,#1", "clear MMU enable"))
221:        p.append((0x08, 0xEE010F10, "mcr 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "MMU OFF -> VA == PA"))
222:    p.append((0x0c, 0xEE113F10, "mrc 15,0,r3,cr1,cr0,0", "C2: CP15 c1 read BACK"))
223:    emit(0x10, mov("r0", FB), "footbridge base")
224:    emit(0x14, mov("r4", DATA), "scratch RAM")
225:    emit(0x18, ldr("r5", "r4", K_LIVE), "C1 liveness, expect 0x11223344")
226:    emit(0x1c, ldr("r6", "r0", 0), "A1 VENDOR_ID, expect 0x1011")
227:    emit(0x20, ldr("r2", "r4", K_SRC), "0x12345678 out of RAM")
228:    emit(0x24, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "TIMER_x_LOAD <- 0x12345678")
229:    emit(0x28, ldr("r7", "r0", T_LOAD), "A2 expect 0x00345678 (the handler's mask)")
230:    emit(0x2c, ldr("r2", "r4", K_CON), "CTRL = TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256")
231:    emit(0x30, st("r2", "r0", T_CTRL), "TIMER_1_CONTROL <- 0x88")
232:    emit(0x34, ldr("r2", "r4", K_L), "load 256")
233:    emit(0x38, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "TIMER_1_LOAD <- 256 => 762.94 Hz")
234:    emit(0x3c, ldr("r8", "r4", K_SEN), "sentinel 0x00FFFFFF")
235:    emit(0x40, ldr("ip", "r4", K_SPIN), "spin budget")
236:    emit(0x44, mov("sl", 0), "drained count")
237:    emit(0x48, mov("lr", 0), "accrual iterations")
238:    emit(0x4c, mov("r1", 0), "")
239:    emit(0x50, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 0")
240:    if disable_during_accrual:
241:        emit(0x54, ldr("r2", "r4", K_COFF), "arm X: CTRL = 0")
242:        emit(0x58, st("r2", "r0", T_CTRL),
243:             "arm X: DISABLE.  Zeroes the counter ONCE and leaves the core timer running.")
244:    else:
245:        emit(0x54, NOP, "")
246:        emit(0x58, NOP, "")
247:    #  accrual loop -- publishes liveness and a fresh VENDOR_ID every iteration
248:    emit(0x5c, add1("lr"), "ACCRUE: iterations++")
249:    emit(0x60, st("lr", "r4", O_ITER), "publish iterations")
250:    emit(0x64, ldr("r1", "r0", 0), "fresh VENDOR_ID read, every iteration")
251:    emit(0x68, st("r1", "r4", O_VEND), "publish it")
252:    p.append((0x6c, br(0xE, CODE + 0x5c, CODE + 0x6c)[0], None, "b ACCRUE"))
253:    #  ---- DRAIN entry.  The harness sets pc here after the accrual free-run. ----
254:    if disable_during_accrual:
255:        emit(0x70, ldr("r2", "r4", K_CON), "arm X: re-enable")
256:        emit(0x74, st("r2", "r0", T_CTRL),
257:             "arm X: CONTROL <- 0x88.  reload_timer_value() does NOT clear the counter.")
258:    else:
259:        emit(0x70, NOP, "")
260:        emit(0x74, NOP, "")
261:    emit(0x78, ldr("r2", "r4", K_FRZ), "0x00FFFFFF")
262:    emit(0x7c, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "FREEZE: LOAD <- 0x00FFFFFF => 0.0116 Hz")
263:    emit(0x80, mov("r1", 1), "")
264:    emit(0x84, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 1")
265:    emit(0x88, ldr("sp", "r4", K_LIM), "drain limit")
266:    emit(0x8c, st("r8", "r0", T_VALUE), "DLOOP: TIMER_1_VALUE <- sentinel")
267:    emit(0x90, movr("fp", "ip"), "reset spin budget")
268:    emit(0x94, ldr("r9", "r0", T_VALUE), "SPIN: read TIMER_1_VALUE")
269:    emit(0x98, cmpr("r9", "r8"), "")
270:    p.append((0x9c, br(0x1, CODE + 0xac, CODE + 0x9c)[0], None, "bne TICKSEEN"))
271:    emit(0xa0, subs1("fp"), "")
272:    p.append((0xa4, br(0x1, CODE + 0x94, CODE + 0xa4)[0], None, "bne SPIN"))
273:    p.append((0xa8, br(0xE, CODE + 0xc8, CODE + 0xa8)[0], None,
274:              "budget expired, sentinel intact -> backlog is 0"))
275:    emit(0xac, st("r9", "r4", O_LASTV), "TICKSEEN: publish the value the tick wrote")
276:    emit(0xb0, st("r1", "r0", T_CLEAR), "TIMER_1_CLEAR -- one decrement")
277:    emit(0xb4, add1("sl"), "")
278:    emit(0xb8, st("sl", "r4", O_DRAIN), "publish the drained count")
279:    emit(0xbc, cmpr("sl", "sp"), "")
280:    p.append((0xc0, br(0xA, CODE + 0xdc, CODE + 0xc0)[0], None, "bge OVER"))
281:    p.append((0xc4, br(0xE, CODE + 0x8c, CODE + 0xc4)[0], None, "b DLOOP"))
282:    emit(0xc8, mov("r1", 3), "DONE:")
283:    emit(0xcc, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 3")
284:    emit(0xd0, ldr("r1", "r0", 0), "final VENDOR_ID")
285:    emit(0xd4, st("r1", "r4", O_VEND), "")
286:    p.append((0xd8, br(0xE, CODE + 0xd8, CODE + 0xd8)[0], None, "HALT"))
287:    emit(0xdc, mov("r1", 4), "OVER:")
288:    emit(0xe0, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 4 -- drain limit hit")
289:    p.append((0xe4, br(0xE, CODE + 0xd8, CODE + 0xe4)[0], None, "b HALT"))
290:    return p
291:
292:
293:DRAIN_ENTRY = CODE + 0x70
294:PROLOGUE_N = 21          # 0x00 .. 0x50 inclusive
295:
296:
297:# ------------------------------------------------------------------ session
298:class Session:
299:    def __init__(self, emul="cats"):           # #545: leg H1 passes an unknown name
300:        self.buf, self.dead = "", False
301:        self.emul = emul
302:        self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
303:        if self.pid == 0:
304:            os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-T", "-E", emul, "-M", "32",
305:                            "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
306:            os._exit(127)
307:
308:    def rd(self, t=0.4):
309:        r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
310:        if self.fd not in r:
311:            return True
312:        try:
313:            d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
314:        except OSError:
315:            self.dead = True
316:            return False
317:        if not d:
318:            self.dead = True
319:            return False
320:        self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
321:        return True
322:
323:    #  #467: an INACTIVITY deadline, not a total-time one.  This used to read
324:    #  `t = time.time()` once and loop `while time.time() - t < timeout`, so a boot
325:    #  that normally takes 40 s and took 90 s on a LOADED HOST failed -- while bytes
326:    #  were arriving the whole time.  Slow was indistinguishable from stuck, and the
327:    #  battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.
328:    #
329:    #  `rd()` cannot report progress: it returns True both when data arrives and when
330:    #  its select() times out quietly.  So progress is `len(self.buf)` changing.
331:    #
332:    #  STRICTLY MORE PERMISSIVE for a run that produces output, IDENTICAL for one that
333:    #  does not -- it cannot turn a passing run into a failing one.  `timeout` now
334:    #  means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth failing on.
335:    def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
336:        t = time.time()
337:        seen = len(self.buf)
338:        while time.time() - t < timeout:
339:            if not self.rd():
340:                return False
341:            if len(self.buf) != seen:
342:                seen = len(self.buf)
343:                t = time.time()
344:            resp = self.buf[mark:]
345:            if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
346:                continue
347:            if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
348:                return True
349:        return False
350:
351:    def send(self, x, timeout=60):
352:        b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
353:        #  `_mark`, not `mark`: gate 6's fresh-mark census keys on that exact spelling.
354:        #  Every other pty probe in the tree writes it that way, so a probe spelling it
355:        #  `mark` has the guard PRESENT and UNCOUNTED -- two of the three counts move and
356:        #  the third does not, a signature that reads like a defect and is not one.
357:        _mark, n = len(self.buf), 0
358:        try:
359:            while n < len(b):
360:                n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
361:        except OSError:
362:            self.dead = True
363:            return False
364:        return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
365:
366:    def free_run(self, seconds):
367:        mark = len(self.buf)
368:        b, n = b"continue\n", 0
369:        try:
370:            while n < len(b):
371:                n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
372:        except OSError:
373:            self.dead = True
374:            return False, 0.0
375:        t0 = time.time()
376:        while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
377:            if not self.rd(0.25):
378:                return False, time.time() - t0
379:        try:
380:            os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
381:        except OSError:
382:            self.dead = True
383:            return False, time.time() - t0
384:        ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
385:        t1 = time.time()
386:        if not ok and not self.dead:
387:            try:
388:                os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
389:            except OSError:
390:                pass
391:            ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
392:            t1 = time.time()
393:        return ok, t1 - t0
394:
395:    def read_words(self, addr, n):
396:        mark = len(self.buf)
397:        if not self.send("dump 0x%08x 0x%08x" % (addr, addr + 4 * n)):
398:            return None
399:        out = {}
400:        for line in self.buf[mark:].splitlines():
401:            m = re.match(r"\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})\s+((?:[0-9a-f]{8}\s+){1,4})", line)
402:            if not m:
403:                continue
404:            base = int(m.group(1), 16)
405:            for i, w in enumerate(m.group(2).split()):
406:                out[base + 4 * i] = struct.unpack("<I", struct.pack(">I", int(w, 16)))[0]
407:        vals = [out.get(addr + 4 * i) for i in range(n)]
408:        return None if any(v is None for v in vals) else vals
409:
410:    def regs(self, which=(3, 5, 6, 7)):
411:        mark = len(self.buf)
412:        if not self.send("reg", timeout=30) or self.dead:
413:            return {}
414:        seg, got = self.buf[mark:], {}
415:        for k in which:
416:            m = re.search(r"\br%d\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % k, seg)
417:            if m:
418:                got[k] = int(m.group(1), 16)
419:        return got
420:
421:    def close(self, patience=20.0):
422:        try:
423:            os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
424:        except OSError:
425:            pass
426:        # Drain through EOF; closing a live master can SIGHUP the child.
427:        deadline = time.monotonic() + patience
428:        eof = False
429:        while True:
430:            left = deadline - time.monotonic()
431:            if left <= 0:
432:                break
433:            if not self.rd(min(0.1, left)):
434:                eof = True
435:                break
436:        try:
437:            os.close(self.fd)
438:        except OSError:
439:            pass
440:        #  #539 (pass 2): BOUNDED reap.  A child that survives the master close (SIGHUP ignored or

476:def plant(s, prog):
477:    for off, v in CONSTS.items():
478:        s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, v))
479:    for off in OUTS:
480:        s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, POISON))
481:    for off, word, _t, _c in prog:

485:
486:def spell_ok(expected, got):
487:    """The emulator's own spelling differs cosmetically from the generator's:
488:    it prints `ldr r5,[r4]` for a zero offset and `#0` rather than `#0x0`.  Those
489:    are FORMAT differences, not encoding differences -- the WORD is compared
490:    separately and exactly.  Normalise both sides so the check keeps its teeth
491:    (a wrong register or a wrong offset still fails) without red rows for
492:    punctuation."""
493:    def norm(t):
494:        t = re.sub(r"\s+", "", t.lower())
495:        t = re.sub(r",#0\]", "]", t)                      # [r4,#0]  -> [r4]
496:        t = re.sub(r"#0x([0-9a-f]+)",
497:                   lambda m: "#" + str(int(m.group(1), 16)), t)   # #0x100 -> #256
498:        return t
499:    return norm(expected) == norm(got)
500:
501:
502:def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
503:    planted = {CODE + off: (w, t) for off, w, t, _ in prog}
504:    bad, covered, ex = [], set(), []
505:    for _ in range(n):
506:        mark = len(s.buf)
507:        if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
508:            bad.append("host died / no prompt at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
509:            break
510:        m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", s.buf[mark:])
511:        if not m:
512:            bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
513:            break
514:        addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
515:        got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
516:        ex.append((addr, word, got))
517:        if addr not in planted:
518:            bad.append("stepped 0x%08x, never planted" % addr)
519:            continue
520:        pw, pt = planted[addr]
521:        covered.add(addr)
522:        if word != pw:
523:            bad.append("0x%08x read back 0x%08x, planted 0x%08x" % (addr, word, pw))
524:        if pt is not None and not spell_ok(pt, got):
525:            bad.append("0x%08x %08x -> %r, expected %r" % (addr, word, got, pt))
526:    return bad, covered, ex

529:def run_arm(label, mmu_on=False, disable=False, accrue=None, max_drain_s=30.0):
530:    accrue = ACCRUE_S if accrue is None else accrue
531:    prog = build(mmu_on=mmu_on, disable_during_accrual=disable)
532:    s = Session()
533:    if not s.wait(timeout=120):
534:        return dict(label=label, status="NO-PROMPT", **s.close())
535:    plant(s, prog)
536:    bad, cov, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
537:    #  ACCRUAL.  The SIGALRM timer is stopped at the debugger prompt
538:    #  (debugger.c:800) and restarted on `continue` (:840), so only free-run
539:    #  seconds accrue -- which is what makes rate*T predictable at all.
540:    ok, dt = s.free_run(accrue)
541:    accrued = dt
542:    mid = s.read_words(DATA + O_DRAIN, 5) if ok and not s.dead else None
543:    #  PHASE CHANGE: the harness, not a guest clock, decides when accrual ends.
544:    if ok and not s.dead:
545:        s.send("pc=0x%08x" % DRAIN_ENTRY)
546:    #  DRAIN, in slices, until PHASE says done.
547:    t0, phase, out = time.time(), None, None
548:    while ok and not s.dead and time.time() - t0 < max_drain_s:
549:        ok, _ = s.free_run(0.5)
550:        if not ok or s.dead:
551:            break
552:        out = s.read_words(DATA + O_DRAIN, 5)
553:        if out is None:
554:            break
555:        phase = out[4]
556:        if phase in (3, 4):
557:            break
558:    r = s.regs()
559:    status = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else ("SURVIVED" if ok else "INTERRUPT-LOST")
560:    end = s.close()
561:    return dict(label=label, status=status, regs=r, badis=bad, executed=ex,
562:                covered=cov, want={CODE + o for o, *_ in prog},
563:                accrued=accrued, mid=mid, out=out, phase=phase,
564:                drain_s=time.time() - t0, **end)

567:#  Arm E walks the drain body in SEGMENTS, each entered by setting pc directly.
568:#
569:#  WHY SEGMENTS AND NOT ONE WALK, stated because the first draft did the obvious
570:#  thing and got 7 offsets of silent non-coverage: under `step 1` the emulated CPU
571:#  advances ONE cycle per command, and DEVICE_TICK is driven by the dyntrans cycle
572:#  counter at one call per 1 << DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT == 16384 cycles.  So a
573:#  single-stepped SPIN loop can never see a delivered tick inside a few dozen steps
574:#  and the TICKSEEN arm is unreachable by stepping, however large the backlog is.
575:#
576:#  This check is about DECODE, not about control flow: it asks whether the emulator
577:#  reads back each planted word as the instruction the assembler above meant.  That
578:#  question is answered wherever the word executes, so entering a segment at its head
579:#  is legitimate here -- and it is NOT a substitute for the behavioural evidence,
580:#  which is separate: reaching phase 3 with a non-zero drained count is only possible
581:#  if 0xac..0xc4 and 0xc8..0xd4 executed for real, in order, during arms D and X.
582:E_SEGMENTS = ((0x70, 12),        # re-enable NOPs, freeze, PHASE=1, DLOOP, SPIN head
583:              (0xa0, 2),         # subs fp / bne SPIN
584:              (0xa8, 1),         # b DONE -- the budget-expiry arm
585:              (0xac, 7),         # TICKSEEN .. b DLOOP
586:              (0xc8, 5),         # DONE .. HALT
587:              (0xdc, 2),         # OVER
588:              (0xe4, 1))         # b HALT
589:
590:
591:def run_drainenc():
592:    """Arm E: every word of the drain body stepped as it EXECUTES."""
593:    prog = build()
594:    s = Session()
595:    if not s.wait(timeout=120):
596:        return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", badis=[], covered=set(), nsteps=0,
597:                    executed=[], **s.close())
598:    plant(s, prog)
599:    bad, cov, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
600:    for head, n in E_SEGMENTS:
601:        if s.dead:
602:            break
603:        s.send("pc=0x%08x" % (CODE + head))
604:        b2, c2, e2 = step_and_check(s, prog, n)
605:        bad += b2
606:        cov |= c2
607:        ex += e2
608:    status = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else "SURVIVED"
609:    end = s.close()
610:    return dict(status=status, badis=bad, covered=cov, nsteps=len(ex), executed=ex, **end)

616:#  fatal()+exit(1); #545 declines that read, so the control that "this launcher notices a dead child"
617:#  now comes from the harness itself:
618:#    H1  an unknown -E name: machine.c prints `Sorry, emulation "<name>" is unknown.` and main.c
619:#        exits 1 BEFORE any prompt.  Rowed on NO prompt AND the diagnostic naming THIS input AND
620:#        WIFEXITED && == 1 -- five different startup refusals all exit 1 with no prompt (measured,

623:#        r6 = VENDOR and r7 = LOADBACK (it really ran), `quit`, then ONE MORE ordinary `reg`: it
624:#        must fail with `dead` set BEFORE close(), and close() must reap exit 0.  A child dead
625:#        BEFORE the prologue finished shows `next send fails` + `dead` IDENTICALLY (measured), so
626:#        the fresh-reg values and exit == 0 are both load-bearing.

630:def run_legs():
631:    #  H1
632:    s = Session(emul=H1_NAME)
633:    prompt = s.wait(timeout=120)
634:    end = s.close()

637:    #  H2

800:    def backlog(A, ran):

# #548 -- corrections to this directory's own dated artifacts

`PRETABLE.out`, `PRETABLE_ADDENDUM_r2.out`, `p1_brief.md`, `p2_brief.md` and `f2_brief.md` are left exactly as they were
written: each file's mtime is the proof of what was known and predicted BEFORE the measurement that followed it.  So the
false sentences found in them afterwards are corrected HERE, each with who found it and how.

1. `PRETABLE_ADDENDUM_r2.out`, NEW ROWS: *"asked for 4 s at 762.94 Hz (3052)  LOAD_RUN or CTRL_ON changed in the probe"*.
   **CTRL_ON is not read by the pinned line.**  The probe prints RATE * ACCRUE_S, and RATE = EMU_HZ / (LOAD_RUN x 256):
   no CTRL_ON term (the Opus seat, table 3).  Revision 4 pins CTRL_ON separately: the probe's `FBDRAIN_TIMER` line now
   carries `load=0x100 ctrl=0x88`, and gate 14 pins the whole line.
2. `f2_brief.md`, section 4: *"A CTRL_ON edit is caught by the bounded and range rows instead."*  **False** (grok, SF2,
   by arithmetic): 0x88 -> 0x80 drops the prescaler, the device runs at 195312 Hz, and under the delivery model the
   measured runs established the residue is 2 -- bounded, in range, PASS.  Closed by the same `ctrl=0x88` pin as item 1.
3. `PRETABLE_ADDENDUM_r2.out`, SECTION 4 CORRECTED: *"Its own budgets bound it (free_run's 20 s prompt waits, the 30 s
   drain, close()'s 20 s drain + 10 s reap)."*  **False** (codex, dedicated and panel jobs, SF2; each with a fake-clock
   fixture): `Session.wait()` restarts its timeout whenever output arrives, so continuous output without a prompt keeps
   it waiting indefinitely -- a 20 s wait was still running at 120 simulated seconds.  No external watchdog wraps the
   probe in gate 14 either.  Recorded, not fixed: a probe-logic change, filed under `fbharnessedits`.
4. `f2_brief.md`, F28: *"REJECTED, and table 3 settled it by measurement: M_lowonly ... is RED."*  **Settled the wrong
   question** (the Opus seat and codex, SF2): M_lowonly also blocks FIRST arms (calloc's 0), so its failure says nothing
   about an UPWARD re-arm.  The mutant that tests it -- M_upskip, the store skipped only on a raise after a successful
   first arm -- passes every row in the battery (the Opus seat, measured).  Filed under `fbbounddebt`.
5. `f2_brief.md`, F5 and F6: *"the repair closes the class"* and *"R5 itself is unchanged"*.  **Both overturned**
   (the Opus seat: M_eq_n2 and M_pend_n0 measured passing everything; codex: a per-timer store mask hidden by R5's
   maximum).  Revision 4: R6 builds every timer in both re-arm cells at distinct rates, and R5 checks every timer.

import ast, hashlib, pathlib, re, subprocess

lines = re.search(r'"'```diff'"\\n(.*?)\\n"'```'"', brief, re.S).group(1).splitlines(keepends=True)
post = {}
i = 0
hunks = 0
while i < len(lines):
    if not lines[i].startswith('--- a/'):
        i += 1
        continue
    name = lines[i][6:].strip()
    assert lines[i+1] == '+++ b/' + name + '\\n'
    before = subprocess.check_output(['git','show','HEAD:'+name]).decode('utf-8').splitlines(keepends=True)
    out, cursor = [], 0
    i += 2
    while i < len(lines) and not lines[i].startswith('--- a/'):
        m = re.match(r'@@ -(\\d+)(?:,(\\d+))? \\+(\\d+)(?:,(\\d+))? @@', lines[i])
        assert m, (name, lines[i])
        start = int(m[1])-1
        old_n, new_n = int(m[2] or 1), int(m[4] or 1)
        out.extend(before[cursor:start])
        cursor = start
        used_old = used_new = 0
        i += 1
        while i < len(lines) and not lines[i].startswith(('@@ ', '--- a/')):
            line = lines[i]
            if line[0] in ' -':
                assert before[cursor].rstrip('\\r\\n') == line[1:].rstrip('\\r\\n'), (name,cursor+1)
                cursor += 1
                used_old += 1
            if line[0] in ' +':
                out.append(line[1:])
                used_new += 1
            i += 1
        assert (old_n,new_n)==(used_old,used_new), (name,old_n,new_n,used_old,used_new)
        hunks += 1
    out.extend(before[cursor:])
    post[name]=''.join(out)

+#include "dev_footbridge.c"
+
+#include <limits.h>
+#include <stdarg.h>
+
+
+/*  The four globals main.c owns.  Linking against every object EXCEPT main.o and
+    dev_footbridge.o is what keeps this detector honest -- it runs the real timer.c
+    and the real footbridge, not re-implementations -- and these are the only
+    symbols that dropping main.o leaves undefined.  */
+struct settings *global_settings;
+int extra_argc;
+char **extra_argv;
+char *progname = (char *) "fbpending_bound";
+size_t dyntrans_cache_size = DEFAULT_DYNTRANS_CACHE_SIZE;
+
+
+static struct machine fake_machine;
+static struct cpu fake_cpu;
+
+static int failures;
+
+
+/*  #442: the identity row's expected counts, named rather than written inline so a reader
+    sees WHICH number moved when a row is added or removed.
+
+    MEASURED, not predicted.  The first draft carried 18/17 from counting the rows by hand
+    and both were off by one -- which is the whole argument for this row existing: a count
+    a human derives is a count that drifts, and here it was wrong before it ever shipped.  */
+#define IDENT_DEEP     28	/*  with R2, i.e. a depth argument was given  */
+#define IDENT_SHALLOW  27	/*  without  */
+
+static int rows;
+
+static void row(const char *name, int good, const char *fmt, ...)
+{
+	va_list ap;
+	printf("  %-4s %-44s ", good ? "ok" : "FAIL", name);
+	va_start(ap, fmt);
+	vprintf(fmt, ap);
+	va_end(ap);
+	printf("\n");
+	rows ++;
+	if (!good)
+		failures ++;
+}
+
+
+/*
+ *  Drive one timer through `n` producer ticks and report the high-water mark.
+ *
+ *  The counter is read back after EVERY call rather than only at the end, because the
+ *  interesting failure is transient: a bound that resets to 1 makes the final value
+ *  small even when the peak was INT_MIN.  A final-value-only check would be green on
+ *  the very defect this file exists for.
+ */
+static void drive(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, long long n,
+	int *peak, int *trough)
+{
+	long long i;
+	*peak = INT_MIN;
+	*trough = INT_MAX;
+	for (i = 0; i < n; i++) {
+		switch (nr) {
+		case 0: timer_tick0(NULL, d); break;
+		case 1: timer_tick1(NULL, d); break;
+		case 2: timer_tick2(NULL, d); break;
+		case 3: timer_tick3(NULL, d); break;
+		}
+		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] > *peak)
+			*peak = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
+		if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] < *trough)
+			*trough = d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr];
+	}
+}
+
+
+/*  Arm timer `nr` exactly as a guest would: a control write then a load write,
+    through the device's own register handler, so the model's own interpretation of
+    the two registers is what is under test rather than a re-derivation of it.  */
+static void arm(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr, uint32_t load, uint32_t control)
+{
+	d->timer_control[nr] = control;
+	d->timer_load[nr] = load & TIMER_MAX_VAL;
+	reload_timer_value(&fake_cpu, d, nr);
+}
+
+
+int main(int argc, char *argv[])
+{
+	struct footbridge_data *d;
+	int peak, trough, nr;
+	long long deep = 0;
+	double f;
+
+	if (argc > 1)
+		deep = atoll(argv[1]);
+
+	fake_cpu.machine = &fake_machine;
+	timer_init();
+
+	printf("==============================================================\n");
+	printf("fbpending OFFLINE BOUND DETECTOR (rung 1 -- a DETECTOR, not a\n");
+	printf("reproduction).  TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP=%d  TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY=%.0f\n",
+	    TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP, (double)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
+	printf("  the most one timer can be ticked in one wall second: %.0f\n",
+	    (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
+	printf("==============================================================\n");
+
+	/*
+	 *  R0 -- the CONTROL that makes every row below mean something.  If the harness
+	 *  cannot drive the counter at all, every bound row passes vacuously.  Drive an
+	 *  ARMED timer 4096 times with the bound deliberately out of reach and require
+	 *  the counter to actually move.
+	 */
+	d = (struct footbridge_data *) calloc(1, sizeof(*d));
+	fake_machine.emulated_hz = 50000000;
+	arm(d, 0, 1, TIMER_ENABLE);
+	drive(d, 0, 4096, &peak, &trough);
+	row("R0 the harness can drive the counter at all", peak >= 4096,
+	    "peak after 4096 direct ticks = %d (expect >= 4096 with no bound)", peak);
+	free(d);
+
+	/*
+	 *  R1 -- THE PROPERTY.  For each of the four timers, and for a spread of
+	 *  guest-reachable (emulated_hz, load, control) triples, the counter must stay
+	 *  inside [0, ceiling] across more increments than the ceiling itself.
+	 *
+	 *  emulated_hz is included in the sweep because it is USER-REACHABLE: main.c:376
+	 *  sets machine->emulated_hz from -I, and emul_parse.c:621 from a config file.  A
+	 *  cap of the form (int)freq with no absolute ceiling is correct at 50 MHz and
+	 *  becomes INT_MAX at the top of that range -- which is mutant m6, and which no
+	 *  row run only at cats' default would ever see.

+		    {    50000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "cats, load 1  (50 MHz)" },
+		    {    63750000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "netwinder, load 1" },
+		    {  1000000000, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I 1e9, load 1" },
+		    {  2147483647, 0x000001, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 1" },
+		    {  2147483647, 0x000000, TIMER_ENABLE,                "-I INT_MAX, load 0 (2^24)" },
+		    {    50000000, 0xFFFFFF, TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256, "cats, slowest legal" },
+		};
+		const int ceiling = (int)(TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * (int)TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY);
+		size_t c;
 		for (c = 0; c < sizeof(cases)/sizeof(cases[0]); c++) {
 			int worst_peak = INT_MIN, worst_trough = INT_MAX, best_peak = INT_MAX;

+				arm(d, nr, cases[c].load, cases[c].ctrl);
+				drive(d, nr, (long long)ceiling + 4096, &peak, &trough);
+				if (peak > worst_peak)
 					worst_peak = peak;
 				if (peak < best_peak)

-
-			/*
-			 *  R5 -- THE BOUND IS THE TIMER'S OWN RATE, AS AN EQUALITY.
-			 *

+			/*

+	 *  @@SELFCHECK@@ -- the SECOND failability control, asking a different question

+	 *  mismatch, so it catches a comparator edited into always-passing -- a state in

+	 *  The token in the row name is load-bearing: selfmutant.py filters exactly it,

+	{
+		int sc_f = failures, sc_r = rows, sc_bad = 0;

+		if (failures <= sc_f)

+	 *  R4 stays as the every-case summary.

+	    "%d row failure(s) above", failures);
+

+	 *  IDENTITY -- the row count asserts itself.  Without it, DELETING a row is
+	 *  invisible: every remaining row prints ok, the verdict token survives, and the
+	 *  gate's `rows actually run` floor only notices a drop BELOW the floor.  This is
+	 *  the constblind shape, and gate section E hard-fails a new differential lacking
+	 *  it.
+	 *

+	 *  a no-depth run red for no defect.  The SELFCHECK block restores `rows`, so its

Disclosure does not make them tested; neither does their existence establish an omitted enumerated cell.
